# TRACK-1 TECH + FASHION PACK (In-Chat Reference)

## 1) Resonance Weather Ladder (the world’s pressure system)
**Core idea:** resonance “weather” is a pressure ecology. It changes perception, tech reliability, travel safety, and social behavior. It is **not magic**; it is a destabilizing field tied to emotion × intent × energy.

### Rungs
1. **Clear** — normal conditions.  
2. **Haze** — subtle distortion; deniable.  
3. **Squall / Ghostwave Bleed** — visible/feelable instability.  
4. **Gale / Front Break** — dangerous pressure fronts; tech degrades hard.  
5. **Landfall / Eye-Wall** — catastrophic; most tech wipes.

### How it looks (three layers)
- **Naked eye:** shimmer/halo on light, horizon wobble, hum/pressure in jaw/teeth, air “wrongness.” It can be subtle (Haze) or undeniable (Gale/Landfall).  
- **AR-aided:** overlays jitter, pixel-crawl, lag, snap wrong, then simplify to audio or wipe.  
- **Reader layer:** described as weather/pressure, not spells. Always tied to human stakes.

### Social behavior under weather
- Clear/Haze: normal life.  
- Squall: people start trusting experience over glass.  
- Gale/Landfall: **calm etiquette + simple signals** become survival tools.

---

## 2) Tech Rungs (how devices fail, on purpose)
Tech degrades in a consistent, graceful downshift:

1. **Rich visual overlays** (full AR/Holo).  
2. **Simplified visuals** (high-contrast, fewer layers).  
3. **Low-res / blocky / static fallback.**  
4. **Audio-first survival mode** (chimes, short bursts, voice).  
5. **Wipe to raw reality.**

**Rule:** the closer you get to truth, the more tech simplifies.

---

## 3) AR (Augmented Reality) — what it is and how to show it
### What AR is here
Late-2010s/2020s-level AR_toggleable overlays_. Viewer-side by default. Not magical. Not omniscient.

### AR modes
**A) Personal layers (default)**  
Each person can choose:
- aesthetic “skins”
- info density
- accessibility mode (high-contrast, audio-first, low-stim)
- channels (MT map, Concord advisories, cultural tour)

**B) Shared space layers (opt-in, space-side)**  
AR-ready spaces broadcast a **local ambiance pack** people can accept or ignore.
Control levels:
1) **Individual** (always final for aesthetics/accessibility)  
2) **Table / seat node** (micro-broadcast for that spot)  
3) **Whole car / room** (macro-broadcast, leader-guided possible)

**C) Leader-guided experiences**  
Conductor/host triggers a synced pack (“route cinema,” salon constellation). Still opt-in, socially encouraged in prestige spaces.

### AR-ready décor / “green screen” canvases
Spaces can be designed to **welcome AR**:
- matte “quiet walls” (clean overlay canvas)
- high-contrast trim for tracking
- stitched ribs/patterns in fabrics
- tracked centerpieces that “complete” in AR
- neutral alcoves meant for shared skins

**Rule:** rooms still look intentional when AR is off.

### Window skins (important motif)
- Subtle anchors along window frames allow **palette/texture skins** over glass.  
- AR **doesn’t change light**; it changes the interpretation on the window.  
- Example: forest skin over desert view → desert flickers through in Squall.

**Proximity-opacity rule:**  
Far from boundary = skin feels solid.  
Close to glass/wall = skin thins; reality asserts.  
Contact/crossing = skin collapses to edges/frames.

### AR failure in a staged space
- **Haze:** slight wobble, edge mismatch.  
- **Squall:** desync by person; pixel-crawl; wrong snaps.  
- **Gale:** auto-fade to calm minimal + audio.  
- **Landfall:** hard wipe to raw glass.

---

## 4) Holochat & Shared Info
**Holochat = 3D Zoom.**  
- Works peer-to-peer and for groups.  
- Runs on:
  - **Personal AR gear** (scaled-down)  
  - **Hubs** (stations, liner/archive decks, airship lounges)  
- Users can share views/documents (“screen share” analogue).  
- In storms, holo simplifies to voice/burst clips.

---

## 5) Channels & Information Ecology
Four channels people trust in different mixes:

1. **Institutional** (Dominions, Technarch govs, official media)  
2. **Concord** (field advisories, hardened comms)  
3. **Folk forecast** (local lived weather/travel knowledge)  
4. **MT — Missing Thread** (public rumor + receipts + morale)

**Era tilt**
- Veil: Institutional primary, MT background.  
- Neon: MT + Folk rise, Institutions lag/break.  
- Loom: Concord + Folk primary; MT provides **receipts + emotional radar**.

---

## 6) MT (Missing Thread) Equipment
### MT Tier 1 — Everyday Reporter (Veil/early Neon)
- AR frames + phone  
- pocket mic/earbuds  
- small stabilizer grip  
- local cache app

### MT Tier 2 — Corridor Field Kit (Neon core)
- chime-nav box  
- portable power slab (solar/scavenge)  
- pocket projector (quick Viewcast)  
- simple audio scrubber  
- physical sticker stack (route/pod/knots)

### MT Tier 3 — Survivor Broadcaster (late Neon/Loom)
- audio-first recorder rig  
- rugged black-box cache  
- analog card deck (maps, witness list, glyph cheat)  
- hand-crank/solar charger  
- optional small mesh relay (local burst only)

**Constraints**
- Not military, not magical.  
- No stable high-res livestreaming in Squall+ unless sanctuary bubble.  
- MT never accesses VT/LT.

---

## 7) Power / Charging Ecology
**Power is invisible in Veil, negotiable in Neon, currency in Loom.**

### Veil
- Normal grid.  
- Power is background.

### Neon
- Patchy grids; “power islands.”  
- Charging becomes semi-social/barter at shelters and cafés.  
- Solar/crank resopunk normal.

### Loom
- Grid mostly dead outside bubbles.  
- microgeneration + scavenged stores.  
- scheduled charging boards at shelters/liners/airships.  
- battery bricks traded like water.

**Priority law under scarcity**
1) survival comms  
2) route truth  
3) triage  
4) threat overlays  
5) everything else dies first

---

## 8) Evidence / Recording / Dead-Drops
**Veil:** truth lives in cloud.  
**Neon:** truth lives in caches.  
**Loom:** truth lives in pockets and hands.

### Modalities
- **Local burst clips** (short chunks survive storms)  
- **Mesh caching** (peer-to-peer in bubbles)  
- **Dead-drops** (Knot lockers, rail vaults, liner/archive decks, airship lounges)  
- **Analog receipts** (maps, witness lists, graffiti records)

**Weather impact**
- Squall: video compresses; audio clearer; mild timestamp drift.  
- Gale: live upload pointless; black-boxes dominate.  
- Landfall: analog or nothing; surviving clips are priceless.

**Truth norm**
- claim without receipt = rumor  
- receipt without witnesses = suspect  
- receipt + witness list = truth

---

## 9) Translation / Localization Under Pressure
**Veil:** real-time AR translation smooth.  
**Neon Squall:** tone/idioms slip.  
**Loom:** macro phrases + human bridges + gestures.

### Layers
- **AR subtitles/whisper translation** (brittle under pressure)  
- **Offline phrase macros** (reliable in storms)  
- **Human bridge** (essential in Gale/Landfall)

**Failure**
- Squall flips sarcasm/sincerity, drops intensity, mangles idioms.  
- Gale/Landfall → simplify speech + confirm-back + gesture.

---

## 10) Symbology (route grammar + convoy signals)
### Core glyph families
- **Blue Thread** (safe corridor)  
- **Frayed Thread** (unstable)  
- **Broken Thread** (segment/hop travel)  
- **X-Thread** (no-go)  
- **Knot** (shelter/rest/charging)  
- **Quiet Car moon** (calm deck/calm car)  
- **POD dots** (convoy pod)  
- **LEAD triangle** (leader rig)  
- **RATED shield** (storm-ready rig)  
- **HOLD palm** / **GO chevron** / **EYES-UP eye** / **AUDIO-ONLY speaker**  
- **CHECKSUM rings** (verified comms)

### Hand signals (mirror glyphs)
- POD UP (three taps)  
- LEAD CONFIRM (point + circle)  
- HOLD (palm up → lower)  
- GO (push forward twice)  
- EYES UP (two fingers to eyes → out)  
- AUDIO ONLY (cup ear + slice down)  
- FRAY (fingertip static wiggle)  
- BROKEN (two-step chop)  
- Optional NO-GO X, CHECKSUM OK

### Dialects
Nicknames/placement vary by city (NOLA “Blue Note,” Vienna “the Line,” Reykjavik “quiet route,” Santa Fe “spider road,” etc.) but **meaning stays constant**.

---

# FASHION / ATTIRE PACK (Aligned to tech/weather)

## 11) Fashion era gradient
### Veil
- Near-modern silhouettes.  
- Prestige travel has mild retro glam.  
- AR anchors subtle/invisible.  
- Status = fit/polish.

### Neon
- **Resopunk street elegance.**  
- Layered modular travelwear.  
- Patchwork with purpose.  
- Symbols worn socially (patches, stickers).  
- Coolness = competence + ingenuity.

### Loom
- **Storm-rated ritual utility.**  
- Universal layering + repairwear.  
- Physical symbols replace dead overlays.  
- Prestige = reliability + calm warmth.

---

## 12) Tier gradient (common → prestige)
- **Common:** DIY survival cool; loud ingenuity (Neon) → heroic repairwear (Loom).  
- **Middle:** quiet preparedness, better materials.  
- **Luxury:** becomes sanctuary-ready minimalism; then quiet warmth in Loom.  
- **Prestige:** Veil glamour → Neon mashup royalty → Loom calm-ritual attire.

---

## 13) Culture fashion dialects (anchors + beyond)
Every culture keeps its voice in **cut, color placement, and ornament density**, even as Loom layers converge.

### Anchor cultures
- **NOLA:** movement fabrics, bright linings, music-coded patches, joy survives.  
- **Vienna:** restrained tailoring, etched symbols, calm austerity.  
- **Singapore:** modular precision; sterile vs DIY split in Neon.  
- **Reykjavik:** nautical utilitarian baseline; sparse symbols.  
- **Santa Fe Scar:** sun/dust layering, bold glyphs, improvisation as status.  
- **St. Louis river belts:** workwear silhouettes, big readable patches.

### Beyond anchors (short dials)
- **Marrakesh:** saturated wraps + metal accents; color as morale.  
- **Rio/coastal belts:** movement streetwear; “party token” survives in Loom.  
- **Mumbai rail megabelts:** smart layering baseline; heritage motifs tucked into utility.  
- **Berlin/industrial blocs:** dark minimal workwear; repair as aesthetic.  
- **High-altitude quiet zones:** wool + calm motifs; excellent boots.

---

## 14) Style archetype roster (for casting)
**Core style engines** you blend 1–2 per era:

Effortless Cool • Dapper Chic • Fly Streetwear • Eclectic Collision • Tomboy/Counterculture • Soft Classic/Romantic • Uniformed Authority • Quiet Luxury • Ritual Traditionalist • Pragmatic Professional • Glam Reclaimer • Ascetic Minimal • Tactical Improviser (gear-heavy) • Nomad/Drifter • Hard Romantic/Noir • Scientist/Archivist • Youth Pop/Swarm • Predator Chic • Domestic Practical • Service Uniform • Office/Bureau Casual • Student Ordinary.

**Blending rule**
- Pick a **Primary Type (Veil)**  
- Choose a **Stress Mutation (Neon)**  
- Keep a **Loom Residue** (one visible habit/item that survives)  
Max two types per era + one persistent tell.

---

## 15) Uniforms & Workwear (professional styling)
Uniforms become more important as AR fails.

### Era shift
- **Veil:** legitimacy + digital badges.  
- **Neon:** field-modified uniforms, bigger readable patches.  
- **Loom:** storm-rated rolewear; physical legibility first.

### Key uniforms
- **Rail staff:** prestige formal → corridor marshals with Broken-Thread + quiet-car marks.  
- **Med crews:** modern scrubs → big cross patches, reflective rescue gear.  
- **Concord:** low-profile tactical → visible role bands (lead/med/comms).  
- **Technarch officials:** sterile uniforms that look “wrong for weather” as failure breadcrumb.  
- **Dominion orders:** ceremonial tailoring → armored menace → sparse storm authority.  
- **Choirless enforcement:** monastic minimal, uniformity as ideology.  
- **Liner/airship crew:** hospitality formal → sanctuary stewards with calm-deck moons.  
- **Shelter/market guilds:** Knot-marked aprons → ration/power steward bands.

---

## 16) Tertiary-casting quicksheet (instant walk-ons)
Pick four knobs:

1) **Place dialect** (NOLA/Vienna/etc)  
2) **One style type** (from roster)  
3) **Era pressure** (Veil/Neon/Loom)  
4) **One tell** (patch, lining color, repeat item, spotless uniform, ridiculous useful mod)

Example generator:
- “Berlin, pragmatic pro, Loom, tell: neat role band.”  
- “Marrakesh, glam reclaimer, Neon, tell: bright scarf with frayed patch.”

# Weapons / Tools Palette Bible (Track-1 Aligned)

## 0) Core stance
Weapons in Concord Saga are **human tools under pressure**, not power fantasies.

They evolve for three reasons:
1) **Resonance weather breaks precision tech**  
2) **Power scarcity favors low-draw / analog**  
3) **People improvise when institutions fail**

So the coolest weapon is the one that **still works in a Gale**.

---

## 1) Era gradient (how weapon use changes)

### **Veil**
**Normal world rules still hold.**
- Modern firearms exist, but are regulated by culture/institutions.
- Most civilians don’t carry daily.
- Concord uses disciplined, professional kits.
- AR threat overlays are clean if used at all.

**On page:** violence feels rare and shocking, not routine.

---

### **Neon**
**Weapons spread because travel becomes risky.**
- Civilian carry rises (especially corridor towns).
- Improvised tools become common.
- Resopunk mods appear (durability, low-power sights).
- AR assist becomes unreliable in Squall → people train for raw skill.

**On page:** fights feel messy, human, and weather-dependent.

---

### **Loom**
**Weapons become survival grammar.**
- Precision tech often dead; big simple tools win.
- Convoys standardize rules to avoid friendly fire.
- Concord kits are among the only “always-works” sets because they’re designed for downshift.

**On page:** you see more **role discipline** than fancy hardware.

---

## 2) Weather impact on weapons (simple rules)

- **Haze (2):** normal accuracy; AR assist okay.  
- **Squall (3):**
  - optics wobble / lag
  - tags can mislock
  - audio + training matter more than glass
- **Gale (4):**
  - visual assist drops
  - electronic sights often useless unless hardened
  - smoke/dust/pressure messes with aim
- **Landfall (5):**
  - most powered aids wiped
  - analog only
  - fighting is desperate, close, and frightening

**Reader-simple takeaway:**  
*The worse the sky, the simpler the weapon needs to be.*

---

## 3) Weapon classes (what exists, no sci-fi)

### A) **Civilian everyday defense**
**Veil**
- pepper spray / batons / knives / legal carry varies by place  
- occasional firearms in certain cultures

**Neon**
- more common:
  - compact pistols
  - shotguns in corridor towns
  - blunt tools (crowbars, batons)
  - “storm knives” (durable utility blades)

**Loom**
- carry is normal but practical:
  - simple firearms if ammo exists
  - heavy tools as weapons
  - shields/door-panels used impromptu
  - “don’t shoot unless you must” norms in convoys

**Look/feel on page:**  
not heroic — tired, necessary, sometimes tragic.

---

### B) **Concord field kits**
Designed to work from Clear → Gale without drama.

**Core properties**
- low-power, low-failure
- redundant
- works in audio-first mode
- simple manual fallback

**Typical kit**
- reliable firearms (service rifle / sidearm)  
- **hardened low-draw optics** that gracefully downshift:
  - Clear/Haze = normal sight
  - Squall = high-contrast minimal reticle
  - Gale = reticle off, iron sights on
- comm-linked **friend/foe bands** (physical + minimal checksum ping)
- nonlethal options:
  - restraint straps
  - impact tools
  - flash/smoke **only in Clear/Haze** (dangerous later)

**On page:** Concord looks cool because they’re calm and prepared, not because their guns are special.

---

### C) **Dominion / institutional weapons**
**Veil**
- formal security arms, ceremonial sidearms in elite orders.
- sharp, expensive, controlled display.

**Neon**
- hardens into intimidation kits:
  - armored elegance
  - suppressed weapons
  - disciplined squads
- they still *try* to rely on tech too long → failure breadcrumb.

**Loom**
- fewer, scarier, close-quarters:
  - durable firearms if supply holds
  - blades, batons, shields
  - ritual/authority appearance matters as much as firepower.

**On page:** authority first, weapon second.

---

### D) **Technarch hardliner / brittle control weapons**
**Veil**
- tech-heavy precision tools, drone reliance, smart monitoring.

**Neon**
- their “smart” gear fails in Squall:
  - over-reliance on sensors
  - brittle targeting
- they compensate with **volume and control**, not better fighting.

**Loom**
- remnants use:
  - jammers (power sabotage)
  - crude guns
  - coercion rigs  
But their identity is still “control through devices.”

**On page:** their tech failure is thematic.

---

### E) **Choirless “Quieting” tools**
Not magic — social and tech suppression.

**Neon**
- portable sound-damp rigs
- crowd-calming audio loops
- noise-filter helmets
- simple restraint weapons (batons, zip ties)
- masks/hood discipline

**Loom**
- monastic storm kits:
  - low-power quieters
  - blunt tools
  - ritual intimidation  
They fight like a **discipline cult**, not a super-army.

**On page:** silence as weapon.

---

### F) **Brightbreak / militia / corridor warlords**
**Neon**
- mixed scavenged firearms  
- improvised armor  
- intimidation aesthetics  
- rely on rumor waves and ambush.

**Loom**
- ammo scarcity pushes:
  - shotguns / simple rifles  
  - spears, machetes, axes  
  - shields from vehicle panels  
  - traps and chokepoints.

**On page:** ugly, human, frightening.

---

## 4) Tools that *become weapons*
This is resopunk cool without new tech.

- crowbars, hammers, wrenches  
- flare guns (clear only)  
- climbing picks  
- bolt cutters  
- oar-poles / walking staffs  
- road-spikes  
- door-panel shields  
- chime-nav boxes *used as impact weights*

**World feel:** survival makes anything dual-use.

---

## 5) Ammo / maintenance reality (tiny but necessary)
- **Veil:** normal supply chains.  
- **Neon:** intermittent; ammo becomes barter; people hoard badly.  
- **Loom:** scarce; firearms used sparingly; close tools dominate.

**One prose cue does it:**
- “He checked the last three shells and didn’t like what that meant.”

---

## 6) How AR threat overlays interact with weapons
**Threat overlays never drive action.** They advise — and fail.

- **Clear/Haze:** minimal friend/foe tags, LOS cones.  
- **Squall:** tags mislock → Concord/civilians trust training.  
- **Gale:** overlays auto-drop to audio pips only.  
- **Landfall:** dead.

**Reader intuition:**  
*When the glass goes quiet, humans get loud.*

---

## 7) Fight tone rules (so we don’t drift into action-fantasy)
1) **Weather determines pacing.**  
   Clear = longer, tactical.  
   Gale/Landfall = short, brutal, close.

2) **No “weak-point scanning.”**  
   Resonance isn’t a combat UI.

3) **Human cost always visible.**  
   Even antagonists bleed, panic, misjudge.

4) **Coolness comes from restraint and competence.**  
   Not body counts.

---

## 8) Beat-bible tags for combat beats
When a beat involves weapons/tools:

1) **Weather rung**  
2) **Weapon class in play** (civilian / Concord / Dominion / Technarch / Choirless / militia / improvised)  
3) **Assist posture** (AR clean / contested / audio-only / raw)  
4) **Ammo state** (normal / low / scarce)  
5) **Human consequence**

Example:
> Gale rung 4. Class: militia + improvised. Assist: raw. Ammo: scarce. Consequence: fight collapses to close tools and panic discipline.

---

## 9) Quick visual “reads” per faction (one-line casting)
- **Concord:** calm gear, neat role bands, nothing flashy.  
- **Dominion:** tailored menace, symbols of legitimacy.  
- **Technarch hardliners:** device-heavy, precision-obsessed, brittle.  
- **Choirless:** austere, uniform, quieting helmets/rigs.  
- **Brightbreak/militias:** scavenged power, intimidation collage.  
- **Civilians:** whatever keeps them alive, often tired and mismatched.

---

## 10) Tiny prose cues
- “The reticle died; he went to iron.”  
- “In Gale you didn’t shoot unless you had to.”  
- “Their quieting rig made the room feel smaller than it was.”  
- “The shotgun was old, honest, and nearly empty.”  
- “Concord didn’t look armed; they looked ready.”

---

# 2) Combat Set-Piece Stress Test (Veil → Neon → Loom + transitions)

I’ll run 5 micro-scenes like before. For each:  
**Beat sketch → Tags → Möbius checks → Fixes (if any).**

---

## Scene A — Veil (Book 2-ish): Clean Threat Overlay, Rare Violence

### Beat sketch
A Dominion checkpoint outside Vienna is supposed to be routine. Seraphine and Lucien are waved through until a junior guard gets a nervous look and asks for a second scan. The AR threat ring in Lucien’s periphery is clean and calm — no red, no spike — just a dull yellow “uncertain.” The guard’s hand shakes anyway.

Lucien doesn’t reach for anything. He steps half a pace sideways, palms open, voice smooth. The guard misreads the move, jerks his sidearm up. Everything freezes for two breaths. Then the senior officer snaps a hard HOLD palm, and the junior lowers the gun like he’s waking up from a dream. Nobody fires. The moment passes with the taste of copper in everyone’s mouth.

### Tags
- **Weather:** Clear/Haze (1–2)  
- **Tech rung:** 1–2  
- **Weapon classes:** Dominion authority sidearms; no civilian carry on POV  
- **Assist posture:** AR clean / advisory only  
- **Ammo state:** normal  
- **Travel posture:** normal road / institutional zone

### Möbius checks
- **Humanity-pass:** ✅ fear + misread drives it, not tech.  
- **Breadcrumb-pass:** ✅ AR “uncertain” ring seeds that systems aren’t omniscient.  
- **Resonance-logic:** ✅ no magic; just social pressure.  
- **Antagonist pressure:** ✅ Dominion legitimacy + human fragility.  
- **Cool/inevitability:** ✅ calm competence prevents violence.

**Fixes:** none.

---

## Transitional B — Late Veil → Early Neon: First Squall Turns Precision Brittle

### Beat sketch
Outside Santa Fe, a militia splinter tries to hijack a supply truck in a rising Squall. Two shooters pop out of scrub with cheap rifles, expecting the lenses to tag targets for them. Their overlays crawl into chunky blocks; the distance is off by a breath. They fire early and hit nothing but dust.

The convoy driver doesn’t fire back. She slams the brake, throws a flare *into the dirt behind them*, and the flare’s glare makes the hijackers blink. The convoy marshal steps out with a shotgun held low and a simple phrase: “You leave now.” The hijackers hear the certainty, see their own shaky glass, and bolt.

### Tags
- **Weather:** Squall (3)  
- **Tech rung:** 3  
- **Weapon classes:** militia scavenged rifles; convoy simple shotgun  
- **Assist posture:** AR contested / unreliable  
- **Ammo state:** low/unknown  
- **Travel posture:** corridor road + threat pocket

### Möbius checks
- **Humanity-pass:** ✅ fight resolves through nerve + posture.  
- **Breadcrumb-pass:** ✅ “smart aim fails first” lesson lands emotionally.  
- **Resonance-logic:** ✅ Squall causes wobble/lag, not supernatural misses.  
- **Antagonist pressure:** ✅ splinter fear, not supervillainy.  
- **Cool/inevitability:** ✅ simple tool + calm wins.

**Fix:** avoid flare use in higher rungs later (we already rule that). Here it’s fine.

---

## Scene C — Neon (Book 5-ish): Close-Quarters, Mixed Tools, AR Downshift

### Beat sketch
A Brightbreak recruit corners Kade in a half-collapsed motel hallway. Neon pop posters peel on the walls like dead skin. The recruit has a pistol and a cheap smart sight he can’t stop fiddling with. Squall pressure makes the sight jitter hard; he keeps blinking at it like it owes him faith.

Kade doesn’t draw a gun. He swings a door-panel shield up from the floor — a ripped-out motel closet door with a RATED shield stencil. The recruit fires once; the bullet punches wood, not flesh. The hallway lights halo; the AR tags die. Kade closes distance fast, drives the shield into the recruit’s chest, knocks the pistol away, pins him with a knee and a breathless “HOLD.”

The recruit is crying before Kade even realizes it. Kade zip-ties his wrists with a restraint band scavenged from a shelter kit. The hallway stays ugly and human.

### Tags
- **Weather:** Squall trending Gale (3→4)  
- **Tech rung:** 3→4  
- **Weapon classes:** Brightbreak pistol; improvised shield + restraints  
- **Assist posture:** AR contested → audio/raw  
- **Ammo state:** low  
- **Travel posture:** urban fracture / off-grid interior

### Möbius checks
- **Humanity-pass:** ✅ fear, grief, improvisation.  
- **Breadcrumb-pass:** ✅ AR drops mid-fight; shield dominance teaches “simple wins.”  
- **Resonance-logic:** ✅ halo + tag death = pressure, not magic.  
- **Antagonist pressure:** ✅ Brightbreak manipulation implied; recruit human.  
- **Cool/inevitability:** ✅ resopunk door-shield is lower-tier cool.

**Fix:** none.

---

## Transitional D — Late Neon → Early Loom: Gale Makes Fighting Short and Awful

### Beat sketch
At a rail sanctuary breach, Choirless quieters move in with batons and damp rigs. The gale outside is chewing the lights; inside, everything is already in audio-only. The quieters don’t shout. They advance in a line, calm as a metronome.

A civilian lunges with a knife. He slips on wet flooring, hits a quiter’s baton instead of a body. The baton cracks his wrist. A Concord marshal steps between them, low-draw pistol out but pointed at the floor. “Back.” One word. The quiter leader adjusts his line — not to attack, to **contain**.

Nobody wants a long fight in Gale. It’s too easy for panic to tip the room into something worse. Everyone knows it. That knowledge ends the moment before it starts.

### Tags
- **Weather:** Gale (4)  
- **Tech rung:** 4  
- **Weapon classes:** Choirless batons/damp rigs; Concord sidearm; civilian knife  
- **Assist posture:** audio-only / raw  
- **Ammo state:** scarce-minded restraint  
- **Travel posture:** sanctuary interior / breach pressure

### Möbius checks
- **Humanity-pass:** ✅ restraint, fear, social contagion awareness.  
- **Breadcrumb-pass:** ✅ “Gale fights are short” rule lands.  
- **Resonance-logic:** ✅ no special effects needed; weather is pressure.  
- **Antagonist pressure:** ✅ Choirless ideology via discipline.  
- **Cool/inevitability:** ✅ calm competence prevents massacre.

**Fix:** none.

---

## Scene E — Loom (Book 8-ish): Landfall Edge, Close Tools Only

### Beat sketch
Near a swamp corridor seam, a militia squad tries to seize a charging Knot. The sky is almost Landfall — the air tastes metallic, and light halos around every moving thing. AR is dead. The only guide is the hum in your teeth and the LEAD triangle on the marshal’s back.

No one fires first because nobody wants to waste a last shell in a world that doesn’t promise more. The militia rushes with axes and a jury-rigged shield. The convoy responds with poles and crowbars. The fight lasts maybe forty seconds — a tangle of grunts, mud, and fear.

When it ends, it’s because the militia captain sees three med crosses moving into the open. He stops. Not out of kindness, out of instinct. In Loom, you still don’t hit medics if you want any future at all. He backs off. The Knot holds.

### Tags
- **Weather:** Gale → Landfall edge (4→5)  
- **Tech rung:** 5 (wipe)  
- **Weapon classes:** improvised close tools; scarce firearms held back  
- **Assist posture:** raw only  
- **Ammo state:** scarce / reserved  
- **Travel posture:** corridor Knot defense

### Möbius checks
- **Humanity-pass:** ✅ ethics under collapse, not heroics.  
- **Breadcrumb-pass:** ✅ med cross respect as last social law.  
- **Resonance-logic:** ✅ AR dead, pressure physical, no magic combat.  
- **Antagonist pressure:** ✅ militia hunger/fear; human scale.  
- **Cool/inevitability:** ✅ survival grammar > spectacle.

**Fix:** none.

---

### Stress-test verdict
The combat rules **hold cleanly**:
- Weather drives tone + duration.  
- AR never becomes a cheat code.  
- Simple tools dominate as the world breaks.  
- Human consequence stays foreground.  

We’re safe to assign specific antagonist “types.”

# 1) Assign faction weapon aesthetics to specific antagonists

I’ll map **weapon posture + visual read** to your named antagonist individuals and key factions. (Not locking canon gear lists — just their *type* so scenes read fast.)

---

## High Inquisitor Marcellus Virelli (Dominion)
**Weapon posture:** **Predator Chic + Uniformed Authority**  
- **Veil:** ceremonial sidearm, immaculate coat; threat is legitimacy.  
- **Neon:** suppressed pistol + close baton; armored elegance; relies on squads.  
- **Loom:** fewer gadgets, more silhouette dominance; blade/impact tool at hip.

**Scene read:** “He is law with teeth.”

---

## Director Han Wei (Technarch hardliners)
**Weapon posture:** **Precision-obsessed brittle control**  
- **Veil:** device-heavy security, smart sights, drone support.  
- **Neon:** still trusts sensors too long; uses volume/jammers to compensate.  
- **Loom:** coercion rigs and crude guns, but always with a device console nearby.

**Scene read:** “He believes control is safety — even when it’s failing.”

---

## Koro Ito & Saeko Morita (Neon anti-resonance populism / PureTone)
**Weapon posture:** **Ascetic Minimal + Crowd-control austerity**  
- **Neon:** quieting tech, batons, restraint gear; public ritual of suppression.  
- **Loom:** monastic storm kits; intimidation through discipline, not firepower.

**Scene read:** “They don’t want to kill you; they want to quiet you.”

---

## Brightbreak (Elias + lieutenants)
**Weapon posture:** **Glam Reclaimer + Predator Chic**  
- **Neon:** photogenic scavenged firearms, knives, charisma-as-weapon.  
- **Loom:** ammo-scarce intimidation; traps, ambush, close tools.

**Scene read:** “Pretty menace that sells violence as meaning.”

---

## 1) Violence calibration: you’re right to flag it
The stress-test fixtures I wrote skewed toward **near-miss / restraint outcomes**. That was intentional for *system proving* (to show ladder behavior without adding canon body counts), but it **can’t become your default mode** in the actual saga, or the world will feel falsely gentle.

### What we want instead
A world in turmoil should show:
- **real violence happening often enough to feel true**
- but **not glamorized**
- and **not constant**, because constant violence numbs readers

So the target isn’t “opt out of violence,” it’s:
> **Violence is present, costly, and unevenly distributed.**  
> People try to avoid it because it’s awful — and often fail.

### Practical calibration rules
Use these as a dial when drafting beats:

1) **Veil (1–3): violence is rare but sharp**
   - 1 “serious violence beat” per act is plenty.
   - When it happens, it changes relationships and stakes.

2) **Neon (4–6): violence becomes common and messy**
   - Expect regular skirmishes, raids, public crackdowns.
   - **Avoidance sometimes works, sometimes doesn’t**.
   - Let **some fights end ugly** even when characters want restraint.

3) **Loom (7–9): violence is near-constant *at the edges*, disciplined in corridors**
   - Corridors/Knots/liners enforce restraint *because they must*.
   - Off-grid zones, siege points, faction collisions? **people die**.
   - Violence is short, brutal, and often confusing under Gale/Landfall.

### A simple pacing heuristic
Per book:
- **Veil:** ~2–4 meaningful fights total.
- **Neon:** ~6–10 fights/skirmishes, with 2–3 major set pieces.
- **Loom:** ~8–12 fights/skirmishes, with 3–4 major set pieces + siege pressure.

Not a rule—just a “does this feel right?” meter.

If you want, next time you drop actual beats, I’ll tag where violence should **land** vs where avoidance is credible, so you get the balance you want.

---

## 2) The all-caps words (HOLD, GO, EYES UP, etc.)
They were meant to represent your **hand-signal / corridor shorthand** — i.e., **simple survival commands** people recognize fast, especially when AR is dead or audio is all that’s left.

But you’re right: **ALL CAPS reads like a UI scream** in prose.

### Better ways to render them
Pick one house style and stick with it:

**Option A — plain text, treated like normal language**
> “Hold.”  
> “Eyes up.”  
> “Audio only.”

**Option B — italic for “this is a known signal”**
> *Hold.*  
> *Eyes up.*  
> *Audio only.*

**Option C — small-caps in your mind, plain on page**
Write normal case, rely on context:
> He raised a hold palm.  
> The convoy went still.

My recommendation: **Option A or C.** Cleanest, most invisible.

Also: you don’t need to repeat the words often. The **gesture + consequence** teaches it.

CHARACTER ENGINE BENTO v3 — Unified Action–Resonance System (UARS)
PURPOSE A cross-trilogy framework that defines how ACTION, RESONANCE, EMOTION, INTENT, and VEIL PRESSURE interact to produce believable in-scene mechanics.
This replaces ad-hoc rules and binds all characters, metas, antagonists, and environments to a consistent system.

I. CORE EQUATION (SAGA LAW) Resonance Output (RO) = Energy × Emotion × Intent × Environmental Pressure Modifiers
Where: • Energy = biological + psychological stamina • Emotion = amplitude + coherence (clarity vs chaos) • Intent = conscious direction, moral direction, clarity • Pressure = Veil density, shard stress, entourage emotion, location instability
RO never produces supernatural powers.
It ONLY enhances: speed, reflex, strength, perception, empathy, endurance, clarity, emotional projection.

II. RESONANT ACTION CLASSES A. INTERNAL MODIFIERS (self-directed) • Reflex acceleration (reaction advantage) • Micro-strength boosts (10–15% plausible) • Emotional resilience (regulation, stability) • Sensory sharpening (clarity, perception)
B. EXTERNAL HUMAN-ACTION BOOSTS • Push, pull, lift, restrain, redirect • Short bursts of explosiveness (like adrenaline) • Directional clarity (fighter instinct sharpened) • Tactical pattern recognition
C. FIELD EMISSION EFFECTS (non-magical, environmental) • Emotional waves felt by nearby people • Stabilizing or destabilizing fields • Resonance bleed that affects crowds differently • Color-light distortion (visual language only)
D. NON-CAPABILITIES (hard limits) Resonance CANNOT: • Throw objects telekinetically • Heal wounds • Produce energy beams • Break physics • Allow superhuman leaps or speed

III. MASTER CURVE: SKILL × RESONANCE GROWTH Growth is not linear. Growth = Usage × Stress × Insight × Time
Tier 1 — Instinct (Veil Book 1) Tier 2 — Control (Veil Book 2–3) Tier 3 — Strain (Neon Book 4–5) Tier 4 — Fracture (Neon Book 6) Tier 5 — Alignment (Loom Book 7) Tier 6 — Convergence (Loom Book 8) Tier 7 — Mastery / Ascension (Loom Book 9)
Everyone progresses unevenly.
Regression is normal under: • Grief • Trauma • Overexertion • Veil pressure spikes • Environmental instability

IV. VEIL PRESSURE & TORQUE (ENVIRONMENT AS FRICTION) Veil Pressure = resistance to resonance use.
Acts like: • friction against movement • drag against emotional projection • static that disrupts clarity • torque that distorts intent
Regions: • Veil Era → Medium resistance • Neon Era → Chaotic interference • Loom Era → Near-zero barrier / catastrophic influx • Post-Mending → Balanced, breathable, regulated

V. CHARACTER SCALING — “NO EQUAL BOOSTS” Resonance amplifies native ability, not replacing it.
Examples: • A trained boxer gains cleaner timing. • A librarian gains faster pattern recognition. • A firefighter gains a burst of lift strength. • A teen Filament gains clarity to not panic.
The SAME resonance spike affects characters differently based on:
	1	Baseline ability
	2	Emotional amplitude
	3	Intent clarity
	4	Environmental pressure
	5	Experience using resonance

VI. EMPATHY RESISTANCE (ANTI-FIELD) Humans can resist emotional bleed by: • grounding rituals
• breathing techniques
• cognitive override
• emotional dissonance
• trauma (unhealthy but effective)
Resistance is essential for: • antagonists
• Lucien
• Elisabet
• hardened civilians in Neon
• metas (because they lack emotion/intent)

VII. METAS UNDER UARS (Manufactured Resonance) Manufactured Metas = resonance batteries with NO INTENT.
	•	Can store enormous resonance • Cannot express it safely • Emit chaotic bleed • Collapse under strain • Cannot ascend • Used by antagonists as living reactors • Fail catastrophically when emotional fields spike
Their existence proves why Intent is required for safe resonance expression.

VIII. COMBAT / ACTION TRANSLATION In an action scene, resonance expresses as:
	1	TIMING ADVANTAGE (micro-second decision clarity)
	2	MOMENTUM MODIFIERS (slightly stronger pushes, pivots, redirects)
	3	PERCEPTION EDGE (reads body language, predicts movement)
	4	EMOTIONAL FIELD EFFECTS • intimidate • stabilize • confuse • embolden others
	5	COST Every resonance action drains: • stamina • clarity • emotional stability
Peak moments ALWAYS extract emotional or physical price.

IX. SKILL TREE HOOKS (FOR PROSE) When describing resonance in scenes, anchor to 5 lenses:
	1	Body breath, heartbeat, muscle tension, stance
	2	Emotion clarity vs chaos, fear vs courage
	3	Light / Color subtle haloing, glints, distortion
	4	Sound hum, oscillation, muffling, sharpening
	5	Environment Veil pressure, resonance torque, emotional density
These 5 define how resonance feels for the reader.

X. TRILOGY PROGRESSION SUMMARY VEIL — discovery, subtlety, imbalance
NEON — saturation, volatility, bloom/riot events
LOOM — collapse, raw resonance, convergence
POST-MENDING — breathable field, selective flow, stability

END OF BENTO v3

UNIFIED MECHANICS BIBLE v1.0
The Concord Saga – Resonance, Action, and Veil Physics System
(Full Long-Form Canon for Notion Storage)

	1	PURPOSE This document defines all mechanical rules governing Resonance, Intent, Emotion, Veil Pressure, environmental behavior, manufactured metas, combat influence, growth curves, and trilogy-wide progression.
It replaces: • all early “resonance is just vibes” drafts • any contradictory subsystem • all previous partial mechanics files
This is now canon.

	1	CORE EQUATION Resonance Output (RO) = Energy × Emotion × Intent × Environmental Pressure Modifiers
1.1 Definitions
• Energy = biological stamina + psychological stamina
• Emotion = amplitude (strength) × coherence (clarity vs chaos)
• Intent = conscious direction + moral alignment
• Pressure = Veil density, shard stress, crowd emotion, location instability
1.2 Nature of RO
RO modulates human ability.
RO never creates non-human powers.
RO is always bounded by the laws of physics but can push humans toward the upper plausible edge of performance.

	1	CATEGORIES OF RESONANT EXPRESSION Resonance manifests through:
2.1 INTERNAL MODIFIERS
(affecting the user’s own body)
• enhanced reflex clarity
• focus sharpening
• micro-strength bursts (10–20%)
• emotional stabilizing
• sensory amplification (but not superhuman)
2.2 EXTERNAL HUMAN-BOOSTED ACTION
• improved timing
• cleaner strikes, pushes, redirects
• controlled bursts of explosiveness
• faster tactical read on opponent
• slightly stronger grapples or breaks
• smoother coordination within groups
2.3 FIELD EMISSION EFFECTS (environmental)
Not magic. Not telekinesis.
Effects are emotional → social → atmospheric: • resonance hums
• emotional pressure felt by others
• disorientation or grounding influence
• color-light distortions
• mild audiovisual aberrations
• crowd behavior shaping (fear, unity, confusion)
2.4 HARD LIMITS (cannot occur)
• telekinesis
• super speed
• healing
• invisibility
• flight
• energy blasts
• phasing
• object creation
• breaking conservation of momentum
Boundaries maintain realism.

	1	VEIL PRESSURE & RESONANCE TORQUE Veil Pressure = the resistance field that restricts or interferes with resonance use.
Acts like friction in physics: • muffles emotional projection
• slows resonance uptake
• increases energy cost
• distorts intent (“torque”)
• destabilizes coherence
• produces bleed fields
3.1 Trilogy States
• Veil (T1): Medium resistance, stabilizing
• Neon (T2): Chaotic, unpredictable, glitching
• Loom (T3): Near-zero barrier + catastrophic inflow
• Post-Mending: Breathable, controlled, stable
3.2 Character Interaction
Skilled use reduces pressure cost.
Poor use amplifies misfires and emotional spill.

	1	RESONANT ACTION ECONOMY Every action has a cost.
Cost drains: • physiological stamina
• emotional stability
• clarity of Intent
• coherence of Emotion
• cognitive processing
High-intensity scenes require tradeoffs: • tactical choices vs emotional expenditure
• physical strain vs clarity
• sustained resonance causes tremors, confusion, nausea, emotional flooding

	1	CHARACTER SCALING & GROWTH CURVES Resonance amplifies native ability. It does not equalize humans.
5.1 Scaling Principle
Boost = RO × Baseline Talent Modifier
Examples: • Athletes get large gains in timing/coordination
• Academics get large gains in pattern recognition
• Caregivers get large gains in stabilizing fields
• Fighters get strong momentum modifiers
• Bureaucrats get clarity + pattern sensing
5.2 Uneven Growth
Characters progress differently: • sudden leaps
• plateaus
• regressions after trauma
• breakthroughs only after emotional catalysis
5.3 Seven-Stage Master Curve
Stage 1 — Instinct
Stage 2 — Control
Stage 3 — Strain
Stage 4 — Fracture
Stage 5 — Alignment
Stage 6 — Convergence
Stage 7 — Mastery / Ascension
No character hits the same stages on the same schedule.

	1	RESONANCE IN COMBAT — SHOW NOT TELL Combat enhancements manifest as:
6.1 TIMING
Microseconds of insight → cleaner movement.
6.2 MOMENTUM
Better leverage, follow-through, redirection.
6.3 PERCEPTION
Reads opponent micro-expressions, stance shifts.
6.4 EMOTIONAL FIELD EFFECTS
Your emotional state subtly affects the opponent: • intimidation
• confusion
• dread
• calm
• solidarity
6.5 COST
Every resonant strike drains: • clarity
• breath
• emotional equilibrium
High RO = high cost.

	1	EMPATHY RESISTANCE + FIELD RESILIENCE Characters can resist emotional bleed:
Methods: • breathing rituals
• grounding techniques
• cognitive override
• trauma (“hard shell”)
• trained disassociation
• humor
• cultural ritual barriers
High empathy ≠ helpless.
Resistance is a dynamic stat that changes with mood and fatigue.

	1	MANUFACTURED METAS — RESONANCE REACTORS Metas = living batteries.
Properties: • store massive resonance
• cannot express it safely
• lack Intent
• leak chaotic energy
• destabilize environment
• amplify shard activity
• are used as weapons by Hardliners
• fail catastrophically under emotional surge
• cannot ascend under any circumstances
Their instability foreshadows the Veil’s structural flaws.

	1	ENVIRONMENTAL MECHANICS Resonant behavior depends heavily on location:
NOLA: • humid emotional density
• flickers, color bleed
• early shard distortions
Vienna: • rigid pressure, cold flickers
• brittle resonance fractures
Singapore: • neon tears, gridwise distortion
• metas “pinging” tower nodes
Reykjavík: • low noise, high clarity
• stable resonance shadows
Swamp: • prismatic fog
• sound-warp
• emotional clarity in stillness
Environment modifies RO significantly.

	1	LUCK, CHANCE, CHAOS VARIABLES Luck exists.
Randomness can: • amplify a resonance moment
• cancel one
• flip emotional polarity
• produce emergent, unpredictable outcomes
UARS does not behave like a deterministic power system.
It reflects real human unpredictability.

	1	MULTI-CHARACTER INTERFERENCE / SYNERGY Group scenes introduce new mechanics:
11.1 Harmonic Synergy
Resonances align → field strengthens.
11.2 Emotional Dissonance
Mismatched emotional states → destabilization.
11.3 Intent Clash
Two characters’ RO cancel or torque each other.
11.4 Resonant Echo Loops
Repeated emotional patterns reinforce amplification.
This allows: • Filament group rituals
• Concord coordination
• antagonist crowd manipulation

	1	TRILOGY-WIDE SYSTEM PROGRESSION VEIL (Books 1–3) • resonance subtle, fragile, rare • pressure medium • small boosts only • early Shard precursors • first brushes with VT
NEON (Books 4–6) • saturation
• emotional instability everywhere
• civic panic
• metas revealed
• large blooms and riots
• deaths tied to resonance storms
• Tahl dies, Echo preserved
• Kade destabilizes
LOOM (Books 7–9) • Veil collapse
• raw resonance surge
• catastrophic storms
• ascensions (Seraphine, Caro, Lucien)
• Tahl’s Echo amplifies for final intervention
• Mending forms the Breathable Veil
POST-MENDING • gentle resonance flow
• Echo Nodes appear
• stable emotional ecology
• cultural integration

	1	PROSE GUIDELINES Resonance should ALWAYS be described through:
	2	BODY → breath, posture, heartbeat
	3	EMOTION → clarity vs chaos
	4	COLOR → hue distortion, haloing
	5	SOUND → hum, oscillation, muffled edges
	6	ENVIRONMENT → pressure, torque, crowd emotion
Never describe resonance abstractly.
Always anchor to sensory detail + character POV.

	1	NARRATIVE PAYOFF STRUCTURE Resonance must: • reveal character • escalate stakes • reflect emotional truth • foreshadow Veil health • tie directly to plot mechanics • pay off in climax through meaningful cost
The system is emotional → physical → environmental → mythic.

END OF UNIFIED MECHANICS BIBLE v1.0

ARCHETYPE 5 — MANUFACTURED METAS Examples: • Silver Pattern Man

ARCHETYPE 6 — CIVIC CHAOS / RESONANCE WEATHER (Environmental Enemies) Examples: • Neon blooms
• Loom storms

Capabilities: • Not fightable
• They alter the rules of combat (visibility, timing, emotional states)

ARCHETYPE 7 — SILENCE & HOPE (PRE-MENDING) Not “opponents” but metaphysical pressures characters must act against.

Hope: • Emotional overload → amplifies despair

ARCHETYPE 8 — POST-MENDING SHADOWS (Optional Future Use) If desired in post-Saga stories.

USAGE NOTES • These categories preserve realism: humans remain dangerous.
• Resonant boosts enhance but do not replace skill or training.
• Opponents force character growth, emotional storytelling, and grounded tension.
• Matches Sudowrite’s strengths: emotional action, grounded choreography, internal conflict.

# Alignment check vs Track-1 (tech / weather / travel / AR)
This is already highly compatible. Here’s the crosswalk.

## 1) Weather ladder compatibility
Your UARS “Veil Pressure & Torque” maps perfectly to Track-1 weather rungs:

- **Veil era = medium resistance** → matches Clear/Haze as baseline with early friction.  
- **Neon era = chaotic interference** → matches Squall/Gale as glitching, emotional volatility, AR unreliability.  
- **Loom era = near-zero barrier + catastrophic inflow** → matches Gale→Landfall dominance, raw pressure, close-quarters fights.  
- **Post-Mending = breathable regulation** → matches stabilized comms/AR and Echo Nodes.

No contradictions. In fact, UARS gives the *physics reason* for Track-1 tech failure and fight pacing.

## 2) “No magic / no superhuman” consistency
UARS hard limits dovetail with our weapons palette:
- no telekinesis / beams / healing / physics breaks  
- boosts stay within **plausible edges (10–20%)**  
- field effects are **emotional-social-atmospheric**, not kinetic

So combat won’t drift into superhero territory even when stakes rise.

## 3) AR / tech interaction
UARS does **not** rely on AR as a combat engine, which is exactly what we need:
- RO is *felt* through body/emotion/sound/light/environment.  
- AR is optional “advisory glass” that fails under pressure.

So there’s zero risk of “combat UI cheat codes” unless later prose choices accidentally add them. (We’ll watch that.)

## 4) Travel posture + combat tone
Track-1 says:
- Veil fights rare/shocking  
- Neon fights common/messy  
- Loom fights short/brutal/close

UARS gives the mechanical **why**:
- cost economy + pressure torque + raw inflow  
- regression under grief/strain  
- group interference vs synergy

So the two systems *reinforce each other*.

# What’s especially strong (keep as canon centerpieces)

## A) The cost economy
“Every resonance action drains stamina, clarity, emotional stability.”  
This is your main anti-power-creep governor. It will keep fights human forever.

## B) Scaling principle (“no equal boosts”)
This is gold for character differentiation in fights:
- boxer = timing gain  
- librarian = pattern gain  
- firefighter = lift burst  
- teen Filament = panic clarity

That means **non-fighters can still matter in action scenes** without becoming unreal.

## C) Opponent archetypes + POV counters
You’ve already built a **tactical grammar per protagonist**.  
This is exactly what you meant by “growth systems in character info.”

It will let fights feel:
- emotionally specific  
- mechanically legible  
- not repetitive

# Minor calibration notes (tiny tweaks, not structural)

1) **Micro-strength range**  
You say 10–15% in Bento and 10–20% in Unified Bible.

2) **Field emission visuals**  
You list color/light distortion as “visual language only.”  
Perfect. Just keep prose grounded: halos, desaturation, shimmer, hum — no “force waves” knocking people over.

3) **Luck/Chaos variable**
Great realism add.  
Just remember: luck should *complicate* outcomes, not replace character choice.

None of these are red flags — just polishing.

---

# Drift risks to watch (and how to avoid)
These are the only 3 places the system could accidentally slide:

1) **Over-describing RO as “power.”**  
Fix: always anchor to **timing/momentum/perception/cost**, not “energy release.”

2) **Letting field emissions become combat weapons.**  
Fix: emissions affect **people’s emotions/coordination**, not objects.

3) **AR or tech becoming tactical determinism.**  
Fix: AR can advise in Clear/Haze, but in Squall+ it lies or dies.  
The *body* knows more than glass.

You already agree with all this, so just a reminder.

# Resonance Weather Bible (Track-1 Addendum)

## Core stance
Resonance weather is **pressure in the world’s emotional-atmospheric field**.  
It behaves like weather: fronts, pockets, lulls, sudden spikes.  
It is **not magic**, and it doesn’t create new powers — it **raises human volatility and disrupts systems**.

Every rung has:
- **Naked-eye tells** (what anyone senses)  
- **AR tells** (what glass does)  
- **Human tells** (how crowds/characters behave)  
- **Safe prose metaphors** (for reader intuition)

---

## Rung 1 — **Clear**
**Naked eye**
- Air feels normal.  
- Light is stable.  
- No hum, no pressure in jaw/ears.

**AR**
- Full overlays stable, high-res, smooth.

**Humans**
- Normal behavior.  
- Institutions still feel “real.”

**Metaphors**
- “Blue-sky day.”  
- “No static in the air.”

---

## Rung 2 — **Haze**
**Naked eye**
- A faint wrongness: slight **heat-shimmer** on edges.  
- Light halos briefly around streetlamps or chrome.  
- Low hum you can *almost* convince yourself is traffic.

**AR**
- Micro-lag, tiny jitter on fine text.  
- Overlays feel “thin,” like the world is breathing under them.

**Humans**
- People get irritable or spaced out faster.  
- First uptick in bad calls / misreads.  
- Conspiracy chatter starts here.

**Metaphors**
- “Air before a storm.”  
- “Static just under the skin.”

---

## Rung 3 — **Squall**
(Also where “Ghostwaves” and visible Shard behavior begin.)

**Naked eye**
- **Noticeable shimmer** across distance (roads, skylines).  
- Colors oversaturate or wash out in pulses.  
- Pressure in teeth/ears, like altitude change.  
- Occasional **flicker-moments** where the world looks too sharp or too flat.

**AR**
- Overlays **pixel-crawl / low-res block**.  
- Tagging mislocks, distance feels “off by a breath.”  
- UI auto-simplifies (high-contrast, fewer layers).

**Humans**
- Emotional spill rises: crowds get jumpy, touchy, brave, or panicky in waves.  
- Bad fights start here.  
- People begin trusting the **body over the glass**.

**Metaphors**
- “Driving through hard rain.”  
- “Concert smoke catching light.”  
- “A city seen through heat off asphalt.”

---

## Rung 4 — **Gale**
(Front break. Dangerous. Travel changes behavior.)

**Naked eye**
- The air feels **thick**; movement has drag.  
- Visible **light-smear** around motion.  
- Sound warps: words feel muffled then too crisp.  
- Horizon buckles; close objects still read true.

**AR**
- Most visuals fall to **minimal mode** or die.  
- Audio prompts/chimes survive longer.  
- Shared spaces desync rapidly; window skins wipe to raw.

**Humans**
- Short, ugly conflict bursts.  
- Panic contagion is real — etiquette becomes survival:
  - speak simple  
  - confirm-back  
  - follow role marks  
- Corridors/Knots enforce calm rules hard.

**Metaphors**
- “Walking into heavy wind.”  
- “A thunderhead rolling over the street.”  
- “Your thoughts are wading.”

---

## Rung 5 — **Landfall**
(Eye-wall / rupture level.)

**Naked eye**
- **Undeniable storm reality**: shimmering sheets, prismatic fog, or air that looks like it’s boiling.  
- Light fractures; faces feel “too bright then too dim.”  
- The hum is bodily — chest, skull, gut.  
- Some people vomit or freeze from overload.

**AR**
- Hard wipe.  
- If anything remains, it’s **bare audio pulses** — then nothing.

**Humans**
- Fighting is close, brief, often tragic.  
- People move by **hand, voice, touch, and known signs**.  
- Afterwards, grief and shock ripple for days.

**Metaphors**
- “Hurricane eye-wall.”  
- “Radiation you can finally see.”  
- “The world screaming without sound.”

---

# Regional tints (flavor, not new rules)

These are **palette + vibe shifts** under the same rungs.

## **New Orleans (humid emotional density)**
- Squall/Gale show as **color bleed**, lantern-like halos, wet air glow.  
- Crowds swing toward **collective emotion** faster (joy → panic → solidarity).  
- Storms feel musical: rhythm in pulses.

## **Vienna (rigid pressure, cold edges)**
- Haze already feels sharp; Squall makes light **brittle and glassy**.  
- People grow quiet before they break.  
- Emotional torque reads as *restraint snapping*.

## **Singapore (gridwise distortion)**
- Storms show as **neon tears / vertical shimmer bands** along towers.  
- AR fails in clean geometric steps.  
- Crowds split into “follow protocol” vs “DIY survival.”

## **Reykjavik / Arctic lanes (low noise clarity)**
- Rungs rise slower; when they hit, they’re **clean but cruel**.  
- Visibility stays higher; emotion shocks feel *cold and sudden*.  
- Travel there is prized because warnings are clearer.

## **Santa Fe Scar / Southwest corridors**
- Squall shows as **dust-light shimmer**, mirage-style buckling.  
- Gale feels like being pressed into sun-hot air.  
- Emotional snap points are common — why the rupture mattered.

## **Swamp / Honey Island (mending zone)**
- Weather reads as **prismatic fog + sound-warp**.  
- Even in Gale/Landfall, there are eerie **still pockets**.  
- Pressure feels intimate, not just violent.

---

# Travel & safety behavior by rung

## Clear/Haze
- Normal travel.  
- People might ignore warnings.

## Squall
- “Blue-line only” travel culture activates.  
- Vehicles downshift to safety modes.  
- Train/liner crews start calm etiquette early.

## Gale
- Off-grid driving = storm chasing.  
- Corridors enforce pod discipline and role bands.  
- Passengers accept AR wipe as normal.

## Landfall
- Travel stops unless life-or-death.  
- Everyone moves by signs and shouted basics.  
- Survivors treat any recording as sacred.

---

# Folk sayings / shorthand (reader-simple)
Use sparingly like seasoning.

- Haze: “Air feels thin today.”  
- Squall: “Glass is lying.” / “Stay on the blue.”  
- Gale: “Keep it simple.” / “Don’t argue the sky.”  
- Landfall: “Get low. Get close. Get through.”

---

# One-page “field card” for you while drafting
When you need to cue weather fast, pick **one from each column**:

**Naked eye:** shimmer / halo / pressure-hum / horizon buckle / prismatic fog  
**AR:** jitter → pixel → minimal → audio → wipe  
**Humans:** irritability → spill → panic waves → calm rules → raw survival  
**Metaphor:** heat shimmer / hard rain / heavy wind / hurricane wall

That’s it. One line can carry a whole rung.

# TRACK-1 WORLD CONTEXT SLAB (Refreshed)

## 1) Resonance Weather Ladder (expanded)
**Resonance weather = atmospheric-emotional pressure.**  
It doesn’t make magic; it raises volatility, disrupts perception, and breaks tech.

### Rung 1 — Clear
- **Naked eye:** normal air/light, no hum.  
- **AR:** full overlays stable.  
- **Humans:** normal behavior.  
- **Metaphor:** blue-sky day.

### Rung 2 — Haze
- **Naked eye:** faint edge-shimmer, tiny halos, almost-hum.  
- **AR:** micro-lag/jitter, overlays feel thin.  
- **Humans:** irritability/spacing, first misreads.  
- **Metaphor:** air before rain, static under skin.

### Rung 3 — Squall (Ghostwaves begin)
- **Naked eye:** obvious shimmer across distance, color pulses, pressure in teeth/ears, brief flicker-moments.  
- **AR:** pixel-crawl/low-res block; tags mislock; UI simplifies.  
- **Humans:** emotional spill waves, skirmishes start, body > glass.  
- **Metaphor:** driving in hard rain, concert smoke catching light, asphalt heat.

### Rung 4 — Gale (Front break)
- **Naked eye:** thick air drag, light-smear on motion, muffled→too-crisp sound, horizon buckles.  
- **AR:** visuals drop to minimal or die; audio prompts survive longest; window skins wipe.  
- **Humans:** short ugly conflict bursts; calm etiquette + simple signals needed.  
- **Metaphor:** walking into heavy wind, thunderhead overhead, thoughts wading.

### Rung 5 — Landfall (Eye-wall)
- **Naked eye:** prismatic fog/boiling air, fractured light, full-body hum, nausea/freeze risk.  
- **AR:** hard wipe; maybe bare audio pips then nothing.  
- **Humans:** close, brief, tragic fights; move by touch/voice/signs.  
- **Metaphor:** hurricane wall, visible radiation, world screaming without sound.

### Regional tints (flavor only)
- **NOLA:** wet glow + color-bleed, musical pulses, collective swings.  
- **Vienna:** brittle glassy light, quiet restraint snapping.  
- **Singapore:** neon tear bands, geometric AR failure, protocol vs DIY split.  
- **Reykjavik/Arctic:** slower rise, clean cruel fronts, clearer warnings.  
- **Santa Fe Scar:** mirage-buckling dust light, hot-air press, frequent snap points.  
- **Swamp/Mending zone:** prismatic fog + sound-warp, eerie still pockets.

### Travel behavior by rung
- **Clear/Haze:** normal travel, warnings ignored.  
- **Squall:** Blue-line only culture, safety downshift.  
- **Gale:** off-grid driving = storm chasing; strict pod discipline.  
- **Landfall:** travel halts unless life/death.

---

## 2) Tech Downshift Ladder
Tech degrades gracefully:

1. Rich visual overlays (full AR/Holo)  
2. Simplified visuals (fewer layers)  
3. Low-res / pixel / static fallback  
4. Audio-first survival mode  
5. Wipe to raw reality

**Rule:** closer to truth/pressure → simpler tech.

---

## 3) AR (Augmented Reality)
Late-2010s/2020s AR. Viewer-side by default, opt-in shared layers.

### AR modes
- **Personal layers:** skins, info density, accessibility, channel filters.  
- **Shared-space layers:** local “ambiance packs” a room broadcasts; people accept/ignore.  
- **Leader-guided:** conductors/hosts sync a pack socially, still opt-in.

### AR-ready décor
Spaces include matte “quiet walls,” high-contrast trim, tracking stitches, neutral alcoves.  
Rooms look good **with or without AR.**

### Window skins + proximity opacity
- Anchors along frames let skins sit **over** glass (don’t change light).  
- **Far from boundary:** skin opaque.  
- **Near boundary:** skin thins, reality leaks through.  
- **Contact/crossing:** skin collapses to edges.

### AR failure by rung
- **Haze:** tiny wobble.  
- **Squall:** desync + pixel crawl, wrong snaps.  
- **Gale:** minimal/audio.  
- **Landfall:** wipe.

---

## 4) Holochat
**Holochat = 3D Zoom.**  
- Peer-to-peer and groups.  
- Runs on AR gear (scaled) or stationary hubs.  
- “Screen-share” exists.  
- In Squall+ it downshifts to audio/bursts.

---

## 5) Channels & Information Ecology
Four trust streams:
1. **Institutional** (Dominions/Technarch/media)  
2. **Concord** (hardened advisories)  
3. **Folk forecast** (local corridor truth)  
4. **MT / Missing Thread** (receipts + morale)

**Era tilt:**  
Veil = Institutional primary.  
Neon = MT + Folk rise.  
Loom = Concord + Folk primary; MT emotional radar + receipts.

---

## 6) MT Equipment (what a reporter carries)
- **Veil/early Neon:** AR frames/phone, pocket mic, stabilizer grip, cache app.  
- **Neon corridor kit:** chime-nav box, power slab, pocket projector, audio scrubber, sticker stack.  
- **Late Neon/Loom:** audio-first rig, rugged black-box cache, analog cards (maps/witness/glyphs), crank/solar charger, tiny local mesh relay.

**Constraints:** no stable high-res livestream in Squall+ unless sanctuary.

---

## 7) Power / Charging Ecology
- **Veil:** stable grid, power invisible.  
- **Neon:** patchy “power islands,” charging as barter/social schedule.  
- **Loom:** power = currency; microgeneration + scavenged batteries + posted rotations.

**Priority law:** survival comms → route truth → triage → threat assists → everything else.

---

## 8) Evidence / Recording / Dead-Drops
- **Veil:** cloud truth.  
- **Neon:** caches + burst clips.  
- **Loom:** pockets + analog receipts.

**Modes:** burst clips, peer caching, dead-drops (Knot lockers/rail vaults/liners/archives/airships), analog maps/witness lists.

**Storm impact:** Squall compresses video; Gale kills live upload; Landfall = analog or nothing.

---

## 9) Translation Under Pressure
- **Veil:** smooth AR translation.  
- **Squall:** tone/idioms slip; sarcasm breaks.  
- **Gale/Landfall:** phrase macros + human bridges + gestures.

---

## 10) Symbology Grammar (simple, physical, universal meaning)
### Glyph families
Blue Thread (safe) • Frayed (unstable) • Broken (segment/hop) • X-Thread (no-go) • Knot (shelter/charge) • Quiet-car moon (calm deck) • POD dots (convoy pod) • LEAD triangle • RATED shield • CHECKSUM rings.

### Hand signals (mirror meaning)
Hold palm • Go push • Eyes-up two-finger • Audio-only ear-slice • Fray wiggle • Broken chop • No-go X • Checksum OK circle.

**Dialects:** slang/placement varies by city; meaning constant.

---

## 11) Transportation (tiers + safety logic)
### Personal / small group
- **Veil:** cars/planes normal, trains prestige-novelty.  
- **Neon:** cars risky off-grid; trains safest long distances; air/sea slower but safer.  
- **Loom:** off-grid driving = hurricane chasing; corridors + convoys + trains dominate.

### Mass transit & prestige
- **AR-enabled trains:** Orient-Express vibe; shared packs in dining/lounge; windows skinned; graceful downshift through storms.  
- **Airships/liners:** slower speed = safer through unstable skies; become prestige/sanctuary travel.  
- **Coach airlines:** exist for color/contrast; feel cramped/risky mid-Neon onward.

**Status cool:** ingenuity at low tiers can outshine invisible luxury.

---

## 12) Fashion / Attire
### Era gradient
- **Veil:** near-modern, subtle luxury, AR anchors invisible.  
- **Neon:** resopunk layering, patchwork with purpose, symbols social.  
- **Loom:** storm-rated repairwear, physical legibility replaces AR.

### Tier gradient
Common (DIY hero cool) • Middle (quiet preparedness) • Luxury (sanctuary minimal) • Prestige (Veil glam → Neon mashup royalty → Loom calm-ritual).

### Culture dialects (short dials)
NOLA joy-color linings • Vienna restrained tailoring • Singapore modular precision • Reykjavik nautical utilitarian • Santa Fe bold glyph survival • Marrakesh saturated wraps • Rio movement streetwear • Mumbai layered rail practical • Berlin dark minimal repair-aesthetic • High-altitude wool calm motifs.

### Style archetype roster (casting engines)
Effortless cool • dapper • fly streetwear • eclectic collision • tomboy/counterculture • soft classic • uniformed authority • quiet luxury • ritual traditionalist • pragmatic pro • glam reclaimer • ascetic minimal • tactical improviser • nomad/drifter • noir elegance • scientist/archivist • youth pop • predator chic • domestic practical • service uniform • office casual • student ordinary.

**Blend rule:** max two per era + one persistent tell.

### Uniforms / workwear
- **Veil:** legitimacy + digital badges.  
- **Neon:** field-mod uniforms + bigger readable patches.  
- **Loom:** storm rolewear + high-contrast bands + calm etiquette.  
Key sets: rail, med, Concord, Technarch, Dominion, Choirless, liner/airship crew, shelter/market guilds.

### Tertiary casting quicksheet
Pick: place dialect + one archetype + era + one tell (patch/lining/repeat item/spotless uniform/ridiculous useful mod).

---

## 13) Weapons / Tools Palette (grounded)
### Era shift
- **Veil:** regulated, rare violence.  
- **Neon:** carry rises; improvised tools common; optics brittle in Squall.  
- **Loom:** ammo scarce; close tools dominate; fights short in Gale/Landfall.

### Weather impact
Haze ok → Squall optics jitter → Gale kills visuals → Landfall analog only.

### Classes
- **Civilians:** simple guns where available, knives/blunt tools, tired necessity.  
- **Concord:** low-draw hardened sights + iron fallback, role bands, nonlethal restraints.  
- **Dominion:** tailored menace + squads.  
- **Technarch hardliners:** device-heavy brittle control, jammers.  
- **Choirless:** damp/quiet rigs + batons, synchronized discipline.  
- **Militia/Brightbreak:** scavenged guns, traps, axes, panel shields.  
- **Improvised dual-use:** crowbars, picks, poles, bolt cutters, door shields.

**Fight tone rules:** weather sets duration; AR never a cheat; cost/human consequence always visible.

---

Your action system (RO equation, hard limits, 7-stage growth, opponent archetypes, character trees) **fits Track-1 perfectly**:
- weather/pressure governs volatility and tech failure  
- no magic powers, only human-edge boosts  
- AR stays advisory and dies in Squall+  
- Loom fights are close, brief, costly

No contradictions detected.

CONCORD SAGA — VISUAL CANON RULEBOOK (v1 UNIFIED)
PURPOSE: Ensure ALL visual effects (color, light, sound, geometry, motion) obey resonance logic, emotional truth, and trilogy progression.
CONTENTS: I. COLOR LOGIC II. LIGHT LOGIC III. SOUND LOGIC IV. GEOMETRY / MOTION LOGIC V. RESONANCE-STATE RULES (Veil → Neon → Loom → Post-Mending) VI. CHARACTER VFX OVERRIDES (how to apply Step 7 rules) VII. ENVIRONMENTAL VFX RULES VIII. PROHIBITIONS (what cannot happen) IX. AUTHORIAL APPLICATION RULES

I. COLOR LOGIC — “Emotion as Spectrum”
Colors ALWAYS reflect: • emotional state • resonance pressure • character function
Color Categories: • Warm Spectrum = connection, courage, emotional ignition
• Cool Spectrum = clarity, structure, emotional filtering
• Prism Spectrum = ascension/Veil-linked filtration
• Black/White Spectrum = containment/illumination
• Neon Spectrum (T2) = rupture, instability, bloom pressure
Trilogy Behavior: • Veil = muted
• Neon = saturated, unstable, flickering
• Loom = prismatic, distorted, stormlike
• Post-Mending = softened, breathable, harmonic
General Rule: Color cannot appear without emotional justification.

II. LIGHT LOGIC — “Resonance → Luminance”
Light expressions follow: • breath patterns
• emotional steadiness
• resonance-intensity
Light Categories: • Diffusion = safety, care, empathy
• Flicker = instability, fear, truth surfacing
• Pulse = intentional truth, emotional ignition
• Beam/Ray = clarity, revelation
• Absorption = containment, emotional suppression
• Prism-Split = ascension and Veil transformation
Light changes must reflect character arc.

III. SOUND LOGIC — “Resonance → Tone”
All resonance-linked sound is: • harmonic (echoes human emotional frequency) • geometric (shaped by emotional fields) • non-musical unless tied to music-specific characters
(The Lacuna, ritual scenes)
Sound Types: • Hum = stability or low resonance
• Crackle = instability, emotional overload
• Overtone = truth, clarity
• Pulsebeat = emotional ignition
• Chorus-Effect = multiple emotional fields interacting
Silence has sound:
void-hum + structural pressure tone.

IV. GEOMETRY / MOTION LOGIC — “Emotion → Shape”
Shapes correspond to: • emotional architecture
• resonance discipline
• character identity
Shape Types: • Arcs/Waves = empathy, filtration (Seraphine)
• Lines/Grids = containment (Lucien, Silence)
• Sparks/Chaotic Bursts = raw honesty (Caro, Kade)
• Spirals/Smoke = grief, modulation (Lacuna)
• Prisms = clarity, insight (Elisabet)
• Angular Blueprints = rational logic (Rex)
All VFX movement must follow: emotion → shape → motion → outcome.

V. RESONANCE-STATE RULES
VEIL (T1) • low-level flickers
• ghostwaves subtle
• light dull, soft
• colors desaturated
• geometry barely visible
NEON (T2) • neon-tears
• hard flickers
• resonance storms
• color oversaturation
• angular distortions
• geometry visible in air
LOOM (T3) • prismatic lightning
• atmospheric distortion
• color storms
• large-scale harmonic pressure
• geometry expresses at landscape scale
POST-MENDING • soft harmonics
• filtered, breathable light
• color halos
• Echo Nodes shimmer gently

VI. CHARACTER VFX OVERRIDES
When character enters scene: • Global VFX rules apply FIRST
• Character-specific overrides modify the effect
Examples: • Seraphine’s prism override softens any hostile light.
• Lucien’s black containment absorbs wild background geometry.
• Caro ignites ambient colors into flicker-pulses.
• Elisabet shapes chaos into clarity lines.
• Rex realigns geometry into grids.
• Kade destabilizes stable VFX into stormlight.
• Lacuna shifts everything toward grief-tonal modulation.
• Silence & Hope override entire scenes during collapse moments.
• Tahl’s Echo softens all colors + light within radius.

VII. ENVIRONMENTAL VFX RULES
ENVIRONMENT reacts to: • emotional density
• civic stress
• resonance pressure
Examples: New Orleans = humid glow, lantern-tears, earliest Shards
Honey Island = prismatic fog, water-ripples
Vienna = cold flickers, fracture geometry
Singapore = neon glitch grids
Reykjavík = clarity halos, minimal distortion
Marrakesh = luminous markets, prism-convergence
RULE: Environments must mirror trilogy arc.

VIII. PROHIBITIONS (Hard Rules)
You may NEVER depict: ⛔ Magic
⛔ Random supernatural light with no emotional basis
⛔ Characters flying, levitating, or teleporting
⛔ Light acting independently of emotional or resonance cause
⛔ Pure CGI-like effects not grounded in physicality
⛔ Metaphysicals doing “spells”
⛔ VT visually manifesting publicly
⛔ Shards reappearing post-mending
All effects must tie back to: • breath
• emotion
• resonance pressure
• intent

IX. AUTHORIAL APPLICATION RULES
When writing:
	1	Start with emotional state → express visually.
	2	Use character override if character is present.
	3	Apply trilogy-state filter (Veil/Neon/Loom/Post-Mending).
	4	Add environmental layer if relevant.
	5	Keep everything grounded, sensory, human-first.

## 3) Track-1 VFX Addendum (integrated, reader-simple)

### VFX Prime Rule
**Emotion shapes resonance; resonance shapes what we see/hear.  
Nothing visual happens without emotional cause.**

### The four pillars
1) **Color = emotional signature**  
2) **Light = resonance volume + steadiness**  
3) **Sound = resonance pressure frequency**  
4) **Geometry/Pattern = emotion’s shape**

### Trilogy filter (how intense it looks)
- **Veil:** muted, peripheral flickers, soft halos, barely-there patterns.  
- **Neon:** oversaturated, tearing light, angular distortions, AR warps visible.  
- **Loom:** prismatic storms, landscape-scale geometry, sky-hum, lightning-like color.  
- **Post-Mending:** gentle halos, soft harmonic breezes, Echo Nodes shimmer.

### Color language (clean emotional reads)
- **Red:** fear / rage / volatility  
- **Orange:** urgency / ignition / pressure  
- **Gold:** honesty / courage / truth-spark  
- **Yellow:** warning / cognitive strain  
- **Green:** grounding / clarity / emotional stability (Elisabet tilt)  
- **Teal:** precision / logic / signal integrity (Rex tilt)  
- **Blue:** calm resonance / civic steadiness (rare Neon)  
- **Indigo:** grief harmonics (Lacuna tilt)  
- **Violet:** VT proximity / metaphysical threshold  
- **White / prismatic:** filtration / Breathable Veil / Seraphine tilt  
- **Black:** containment → or collapse/void at overload  
**Rule:** color is never “aura for cool.” It’s state.

### Light behaviors (what light *does*)
- **Flicker:** instability / fear / truth surfacing  
- **Lantern-glow / diffusion:** care / safety / empathy  
- **Pulse-beats:** deliberate truth / ignition  
- **Absorption/dimming:** containment / suppression  
- **Prism-split:** ascension / Veil transformation  
**Rule:** light changes track arc movement.

### Sound signatures (what you *hear/feel*)
- **Low hum:** density / shard-adjacent pressure  
- **Mid hum:** emotional proximity between people  
- **High hum:** bloom onset / big pressure front  
- **Crack:** emotional snap / rupture moment  
- **Whine:** tech strain under load (Neon/Loom)  
- **Static:** VT instability  
- **Silence-tone:** void-hum + structural pressure  
**Rule:** sound replaces sparkle FX.

### Geometry / motion (what shapes appear)
- **Threads / knots:** human connection, Filaments, civic clusters  
- **Lines / grids:** logic, structure, Technarch, Rex-tilt  
- **Fractures:** shard stress, brittle failure  
- **Spirals / smoke:** overload, grief modulation, Kade/Caro volatility  
- **Concentric rings:** stabilizing field moments (Seraphine/Elisabet)  
- **Prismatic planes:** Breathable Veil architecture (Loom climax only)  
**Rule:** shape follows emotion → motion → outcome.

### Character overrides (ambient first, then person)
- **Seraphine:** prismatic filtration softens hostile fields; rings/still pockets.  
- **Lucien:** black containment absorbs wild geometry; quiet zones.  
- **Caro:** opal/gold truth-sparks; close-range ignition.  
- **Elisabet:** green-lavender clarity wash; chaos → pattern.  
- **Rex:** teal linework; stabilizing logic grids.  
- **Kade:** red-orange storm arcs; instability amplifier.  
- **Lacuna:** indigo grief-smoke + trumpet harmonic tone.  
- **Tahl (Echo):** gold-rose soft lantern warmth, comforting radius.  
- **Silence/Hope (old):** can override whole scenes at collapse peaks.  
**Rule:** never “glow for aesthetics.” Always arc-state.

### Prohibitions (hard stops)
Never show:
- magic / spells  
- random glow with no emotional cause  
- flight/teleport/telekinesis/healing beams  
- VT visuals in public  
- MT causing supernatural visuals  
- Filaments making prismatic planes  
- manufactured metas producing emotional light (they glitch geometry only)  
- shards reappearing post-mending
