# TRACK-1 WORLD CONTEXT MEGA-SLAB (Hybrid-VFX Final)

## 1) Resonance Weather Ladder (expanded)
**Resonance weather = atmospheric/emotional pressure.**  
No magic, no new powers — it raises volatility, disrupts perception, and breaks tech.

### Rung 1 — Clear
- **Naked eye:** normal air/light, no hum.  
- **AR:** full overlays stable.  
- **Humans:** normal behavior.  
- **Metaphor:** blue-sky day.

### Rung 2 — Haze
- **Naked eye:** faint edge-shimmer, tiny halos, almost-hum.  
- **AR:** micro-lag/jitter, overlays feel thin.  
- **Humans:** irritability/spacing, first misreads.  
- **Metaphor:** air before rain, static under skin.

### Rung 3 — Squall (Ghostwaves begin)
- **Naked eye:** obvious shimmer across distance, color pulses, pressure in teeth/ears, brief flicker-moments.  
- **AR:** pixel-crawl/low-res block; tags mislock; UI simplifies.  
- **Humans:** emotional spill waves, skirmishes start, body > glass.  
- **Metaphor:** hard rain, concert smoke catching light, asphalt heat.

### Rung 4 — Gale (Front break)
- **Naked eye:** thick air drag, light-smear on motion, muffled→too-crisp sound, horizon buckles.  
- **AR:** visuals drop to minimal or die; audio prompts survive longest; window skins wipe.  
- **Humans:** short ugly conflict bursts; calm etiquette + simple signals needed.  
- **Metaphor:** walking into heavy wind, thunderhead overhead.

### Rung 5 — Landfall (Eye-wall)
- **Naked eye:** prismatic fog/boiling air, fractured light, full-body hum, nausea/freeze risk.  
- **AR:** hard wipe; maybe bare audio pips then nothing.  
- **Humans:** close, brief, tragic fights; move by touch/voice/signs.  
- **Metaphor:** hurricane wall, visible radiation, world screaming without sound.

### Regional tints (flavor only)
- **NOLA:** wet glow + color bleed, musical pulses, collective swings.  
- **Vienna:** cold brittle flickers, restraint snapping.  
- **Singapore:** neon tear bands, geometric AR failure, protocol vs DIY split.  
- **Reykjavik/Arctic:** slower rise, clean cruel fronts, clearer warnings.  
- **Santa Fe Scar:** mirage-buckling dust light, hot-air press, frequent snap points.  
- **Swamp/Mending zone:** prismatic fog + sound-warp, eerie still pockets.

### Travel behavior by rung
Clear/Haze normal → Squall Blue-line only → Gale strict pods/role bands → Landfall travel stops unless life/death.

---

## 2) Tech Downshift Ladder
Tech always degrades the same way:

1. Rich visual overlays (full AR/Holo)  
2. Simplified visuals (fewer layers)  
3. Low-res / pixel / static fallback  
4. Audio-first survival mode  
5. Wipe to raw reality

**Rule:** closer to pressure/truth → simpler tech.

---

## 3) AR (Augmented Reality)
Late-2010s/2020s AR. Viewer-side by default, opt-in shared layers.

### AR modes
- **Personal:** skins, density, accessibility, channel filters.  
- **Shared-space:** local ambiance pack a room broadcasts; people accept/ignore.  
- **Leader-guided:** host/conductor syncs a pack socially; still opt-in.

### AR-ready décor
Quiet walls, tracking trim, stitched ribs, neutral alcoves.  
Rooms look intentional **with or without AR.**

### Window skins + proximity opacity
- Anchors along frames let skins sit **over** glass (don’t change light).  
- **Far:** skin opaque. **Near:** skin thins. **Touch/cross:** skin collapses to edges.

### AR failure by rung
Haze wobble → Squall pixel-crawl/wrong snaps → Gale minimal/audio → Landfall wipe.

---

## 4) Holochat
**Holochat = 3D Zoom.**
- P2P and groups.  
- Runs on AR gear (scaled) or hubs.  
- “Screen share” exists.  
- In Squall+ it downshifts to audio/bursts.

---

## 5) Channels & Information Ecology
Four trust streams:
1) **Institutional** (Dominions/Technarch/media)  
2) **Concord** (hardened advisories)  
3) **Folk forecast** (corridor lived truth)  
4) **MT / Missing Thread** (receipts + morale)

**Era tilt:** Veil institutional → Neon MT+Folk rise → Loom Concord+Folk primary; MT emotional radar.

---

## 6) MT Equipment
- **Veil/early Neon:** AR frames/phone, pocket mic, stabilizer grip, cache app.  
- **Neon corridor kit:** chime-nav box, power slab, pocket projector, audio scrubber, stickers.  
- **Late Neon/Loom:** audio-first recorder, rugged black-box cache, analog cards (maps/witness/glyphs), crank/solar charger, tiny local mesh relay.

**Constraint:** no stable high-res livestream in Squall+ unless sanctuary.

---

## 7) Power / Charging Ecology
- **Veil:** stable grid background.  
- **Neon:** “power islands,” charging becomes barter/social.  
- **Loom:** power = currency; microgeneration + posted rotations.

**Priority:** survival comms → route truth → triage → threat assists → everything else.

---

## 8) Evidence / Recording / Dead-Drops
- **Veil:** cloud truth.  
- **Neon:** caches + burst clips.  
- **Loom:** pockets + analog receipts.

Modes: burst clips, peer caching, dead-drops (Knots/rails/liners/airships), analog maps + witness lists.  
Storm impact: Squall compresses video; Gale kills live upload; Landfall analog only.

---

## 9) Translation Under Pressure
- **Veil:** smooth AR translation.  
- **Squall:** idioms/tone slip.  
- **Gale/Landfall:** phrase macros + human bridge + gestures.

---

## 10) Symbology Grammar
Universal meaning, local slang.

Glyphs: Blue Thread (safe) • Frayed (unstable) • Broken (segment/hop) • X-Thread (no-go) • Knot (shelter/charge) • Quiet-car moon • POD dots • LEAD triangle • RATED shield • CHECKSUM rings.

Hand signals mirror glyphs: hold palm • go push • eyes-up • audio-only • fray wiggle • broken chop • no-go X • checksum OK.

---

## 11) Transportation (tiers + safety logic)
### Personal / small group
- **Veil:** cars/planes normal, trains prestige-novel.  
- **Neon:** cars risky off-grid; trains safest long distances; sea/airships slower but safer.  
- **Loom:** off-grid driving = hurricane chasing; corridors + convoys + trains dominate.

### Mass transit & prestige
- **AR-enabled trains:** Orient-Express vibe; shared packs in cars; windows skinned; graceful downshift through storms.  
- **Airships/liners:** slower speed = safer; become prestige/sanctuary travel.  
- **Coach airlines:** used for contrast; feel cramped/risky mid-Neon onward.

**Status cool:** low-tier ingenuity can outshine invisible luxury.

---

## 12) Fashion / Attire
### Era gradient
- **Veil:** near-modern, subtle luxury.  
- **Neon:** resopunk layering, patchwork with purpose, symbols social.  
- **Loom:** storm-rated repairwear; physical legibility > AR.

### Tier gradient
Common DIY hero cool • Middle quiet preparedness • Luxury sanctuary minimal • Prestige Veil glam → Neon mashup royalty → Loom calm-ritual.

### Culture dialects (short dials)
NOLA joy-linings • Vienna restrained tailoring • Singapore modular precision • Reykjavik nautical utilitarian • Santa Fe bold survival glyphs • Marrakesh saturated wraps • Rio movement streetwear • Mumbai rail layering • Berlin dark minimal repair • High-altitude wool calm.

### Style archetype roster
Effortless cool • dapper chic • fly streetwear • eclectic collision • tomboy/counterculture • soft classic • uniformed authority • quiet luxury • ritual traditionalist • pragmatic pro • glam reclaimer • ascetic minimal • tactical improvisor • nomad/drifter • noir elegance • scientist/archivist • youth pop • predator chic • domestic practical • service uniform • office casual • student ordinary.

Blend rule: max two per era + one persistent tell.

### Uniforms / workwear
Veil legitimacy + digital badges → Neon field-mods + readable patches → Loom storm rolewear + calm-etiquette bands.  
Key sets: rail, med, Concord, Technarch, Dominion, Choirless, liner/airship, shelter guilds.

### Tertiary casting quicksheet
Place dialect + one archetype + era + one tell (patch/lining/repeat item/spotless uniform/ridiculous useful mod).

---

## 13) Weapons / Tools Palette
### Era shift
- **Veil:** regulated, rare violence.  
- **Neon:** carry rises; improvised tools common; optics brittle in Squall.  
- **Loom:** ammo scarce; close tools dominate; fights short in Gale/Landfall.

### Classes
- **Civilians:** simple guns where legal/supplied, knives/blunt tools, tired necessity.  
- **Concord:** low-draw hardened sights + iron fallback, role bands, restraints.  
- **Dominion:** tailored menace + elite squads.  
- **Technarch hardliners:** device-heavy brittle control + jammers.  
- **Choirless:** damp/quiet rigs + batons, synchronized discipline.  
- **Militia/Brightbreak:** scavenged guns, traps, axes, panel shields.  
- **Improvised dual-use:** crowbars, picks, poles, bolt cutters, door shields.

Fight tone: weather sets duration; AR never a cheat; cost/human consequence visible.

RESONANCE — VISUAL GRAMMAR
Base Rules: • resonance is always sensory-grounded
• light, sound, pressure, and emotional tone are primary vectors
• resonance never behaves like magic or particles — always emotional physics
Common Visual Cues: • faint color halo around individuals at peak emotion

SHARDS — VISUAL CANON
Shards = fractures in the old Veil.
They never appear as objects; always environmental deformations.
Universal Traits: • localized distortion of light
• temperature drop
• desaturation OR hyper-saturation
• low-frequency hum (feels in chest)

Shard Types (visual): Flickers: • few-second distortions
• quick ripple of air + muted color shift

• shimmering bands like heat haze

Fractures: • multi-minute environmental warp

Ruptures: • catastrophic
• sky discoloration
• prismatic lightning
• ground-level ripple shockwaves
Post-Mending: • all shards become Echo Nodes: gentle light pools, no danger

BLOOM STORMS — VISUAL CANON
The signature major environmental event of Neon and Loom.
Appearance: • sky pulses like a heartbeat
• neon tears in clouds
• prismatic lightning (Loom)
• wind distortions bending sound
Ground Effects: • flicker halos around objects
• visible emotional “color weather”
• AR/holochat freeze in mid-frame
Human Effects: • emotional bleed-through visible in eyes
• rapid color flashes tied to emotion

VT (VEILTHREAD) — VISUAL PRESENCE
VT is metaphysical; never public; only visible to Silence, Hope, Tahl (post slip), Lucien, Caro, Seraphine.
Visual Grammar: • black/white prismatic triangles
• soft geometric flickers
• thin fractal lines connecting emotional intent
• subtle heartbeat-shaped pulses
Rules: • VT never glitches like tech
• always quiet, deliberate, geometric
• a VT flicker = emotional truth revealed

MT (MISSING THREAD) — VISUAL PRESENCE
A mortal broadcast system; ALWAYS messy, human.
Visual Grammar: • chaotic text overlays

VEIL STATES — VISUAL CANON

	1	OLD VEIL (T1 early) • invisible wall • occasional light flicker at shard points • rare emotional warps

	3	BROKEN VEIL (T2) • AR tearing • color storms • visible bloom vents

	5	BREATHABLE VEIL (Post-Mending) • soft sky shimmer at sunrise/sunset • faint inhale/exhale light on horizon • Echo Nodes glow gently at night

## 2) The real conflicts with Hybrid-final
There are only **three** meaningful mismatches:

### A) Emotional color mapping
Hybrid-final spectrum says:

- **Blue = clarity / truth-breath**

Your older canon says:

- **Green** should be used when the field is *under load but being processed* (Elisabet moments often *touch* green, but her *outcome* can be calm).

### B) VT visual grammar
Older canon: “black/white prismatic triangles” + fractal lines.

- VT is **violet-white planar geometry**, inward-bending light, layered slow sound.  
- Violet is the VT proximity color.  
- Triangles are *fine as a motif* if they stay planar, quiet, and violet-leaning, not MT-style icons.

**Reconciliation:**  
Keep the **triangle motif** as a *shape family*, but tint it VT-correct:
- **VT triangles = violet-white, planar, quiet, intimate**  
- NOT black/white “sharp UI triangles.”  
Black can still appear inside VT when Silence proximity dominates, but violet is the tell.

### C) Resonance intensity scale vs weather ladder
Older canon has a 1–6 “Resonance Intensity Scale.”  
Track-1 now uses **weather rungs 1–5** as the public, lived ladder.

**Reconciliation:**  
Treat the intensity scale as *internal / descriptive* (micro → collapse), and map it to rungs:

- Scale 1–2 (micro-hum → color flicker) ≈ **Clear/Haze**  
- Scale 3 (echo bleed) ≈ **Squall**  
- Scale 4 (bloom-pressure distortion) ≈ **Gale**  
- Scale 5–6 (full bloom → collapse distortions) ≈ **Landfall / Loom catastrophic fronts**

So: **we keep the scale**, but it’s a writer-side lens, not a rival system.

# PALETTE / VISUAL LANGUAGE BIBLE v1 (Track-1)

## Purpose
Define the saga’s **visual temperature** so every scene:
- feels like the same world
- drifts cleanly Veil → Neon → Loom → Post-Mending
- earns awe/coolness without “gear porn” or sci-fi sheen
- stays readable under Hybrid-final VFX rules

This is **not** a shot list. It’s the *color + light + material vibe lane*.

---

## 0) Master Visual Rules (always on)
1) **Human faces first.** Palette serves emotion, not vice versa.  
2) **Resonance color is additive, not a base coat.**  
   Base palette stays physical; resonance overlays *bend it*.  
3) **Coolness comes from contrast + restraint.**  
   One bold thing per frame/paragraph max.  
4) **Low tier can be cooler than luxury.**  
   Ingenuity/mashup > invisible tech.  
5) **Weather governs saturation.**  
   Clear/Haze = stable; Squall+ = flicker skew; Gale+ = downshift to raw.

---

## 1) Trilogy Base Palettes (setting “climate”)

### **Veil (Books 1–3) — Latent Tension / Subtle Uncanny**
**Base temperature:** muted modern realism with a faint antique drag.  
- **Color:** soft neutrals, worn stone, cigarette-paper whites, deep wine accents.  
- **Light:** natural and grounded; occasional lantern-glow in humidity.  
- **Texture:** leather, cotton, old metal, glass, damp brick, paper signage.  
- **Resonance overlay behavior:** rare, peripheral flickers; color stays muted.

**Reader feel:** “Our world, but the air is holding its breath.”

---

### **Neon (Books 4–6) — Fracture / Overexposed Emotion**
**Base temperature:** saturated resopunk, beautiful and unstable.  
- **Color:** high-chroma primaries + dirty neons; blackened shadows; acid pastels in pockets.  
- **Light:** neon tears, hard flickers, blown highlights; AR skin-colors that *don’t fully match reality.*  
- **Texture:** patchwork fabric, duct-tape glam, exposed ribs, scratched acrylic, heat-stained metal.  
- **Resonance overlay behavior:** frequent, angular, oversaturated; *color storms* common.

**Reader feel:** “The world is too loud to look at straight.”

---

### **Loom (Books 7–9) — Collapse / Mythic Weather**
**Base temperature:** storm-scale contrast, rawness with prismatic intrusions.  
- **Color:** earth, rust, bone, soot, swamp-green shadow; prismatic bursts in peaks only.  
- **Light:** breathing horizons, prismatic lightning, deep silhouettes; sanctuaries feel like candles in a cave.  
- **Texture:** waxed canvas, scavenged armor panels, mud-sheen, rope, carved wood, analog signage.  
- **Resonance overlay behavior:** mythic but *not pretty for pretty’s sake*; tied to pressure fronts.

**Reader feel:** “Everything is stripped to meaning.”

---

### **Post-Mending (Epilogue / future) — Breathable Clarity**
**Base temperature:** gentle warmth, washed clean, not utopian.  
- **Color:** softened spectrum, warm neutrals, quiet blues/whites; sunset-prism only at nodes.  
- **Light:** faint inhale/exhale shimmer; Echo Nodes glow subtly.  
- **Texture:** repaired-but-settled materials; less patchwork, more calm craftsmanship.  
- **Resonance overlay behavior:** subtle harmonic halos, never stormy.

**Reader feel:** “The world learned how to breathe again.”

---

## 2) Anchor City Visual Dialects (base lanes)

These are **base physical palettes** before resonance overlays.

### **New Orleans — Humid Lantern-Joy**
- **Color:** river-mud browns, moss greens, brass golds, saturated jewel accents.  
- **Light:** wet haloing, lantern diffusion, warm interior spill.  
- **Texture:** wrought iron, beadwork, peeling paint, sweat sheen.  
- **Neon drift:** joy colors sharpen into bright bleed; streets glow like a festival on the edge.

### **Vienna — Cold Bureaucratic Gothic**
- **Color:** slate, bone, burgundy, black lacquer, pale gold trim.  
- **Light:** crisp winter light, brittle reflections.  
- **Texture:** old stone, velvet, polished wood, archival paper.  
- **Neon drift:** flicker fractures feel like stained glass cracking.

### **Singapore — Vertical Precision Neon**
- **Color:** clean whites/charcoal base with razor neon lines, teal logic accents.  
- **Light:** grid-clean until it tears; vertical shimmer bands.  
- **Texture:** composite panels, glass towers, clean modular wear.  
- **Neon drift:** AR warps into lattices; the city looks “too engineered to fail” — then does.

### **Reykjavik / Arctic Lanes — Minimal Clarity**
- **Color:** snow, basalt, geothermal steam blues; aurora greens only in strain moments.  
- **Light:** big quiet skies, clean contrasts.  
- **Texture:** wool, saltwood, metal softened by wind.  
- **Neon drift:** storms read *cold and surgical.*

### **Marrakesh — Prism Market Convergence**
- **Color:** saturated spice spectrum (saffron, cobalt, brick, mint), sun-white dust.  
- **Light:** glittering ribbons, warm haze.  
- **Texture:** woven cloth, clay, brass, mosaic.  
- **Neon drift:** prism-dust glints become credible bloom previews.

### **Santa Fe Scar — Mirage Pressure**
- **Color:** adobe rust, desaturated turquoise, sun-bleached bone.  
- **Light:** heat-mirage buckling, hard noon glare.  
- **Texture:** sand-scratched metal, leather, tire-rubber, paper-posters.  
- **Neon drift:** colors snap violently between washed-out and oversaturated.

### **Honey Island Swamp — Prismatic Stillness**
- **Color:** black water, moss green shadows, fog-white, occasional prism flare.  
- **Light:** mist diffusion; geometry visible at peaks.  
- **Texture:** wet wood, rope, mud sheen, insect-hush.  
- **Loom drift:** becomes the visual “instrument panel” of the Veil.

---

## 3) Weather → Palette Modifiers (quick cues)
Use **one modifier per scene** unless it’s a climax.

- **Haze:** soften edges, reduce contrast, add slight haloing.  
- **Squall:** oversaturate then wash; pixelated AR colors; light tears begin.  
- **Gale:** crush the palette toward raw earth/metal; prismatic flashes feel dangerous, not pretty.  
- **Landfall:** base palette almost disappears under storm geometry; after, everything looks **gray-tired**.

---

## 4) Tech-Tier Coolness Rules
To get “McConaughey effortless cool” + “Jobs inevitability”:

### **Common / DIY Cool**
- visible repairs, clever hacks, patch-beauty  
- mashups that feel *earned*  
- AR skins that are charmingly low-res when they work  
**Cool source:** ingenuity, personality, humor.

### **Middle / Prepared Cool**
- quiet competence, modular gear, calm palette  
- “boring on purpose” that reads safe  
**Cool source:** reliability and calm.

### **Luxury / Sanctuary Cool**
- minimal lines, soft light, almost no visible tech  
- **one** iconic flourish (a coat lining, a window skin, a rail lounge motif)  
**Risk:** invisible = boring.  
**Fix:** give luxury a **human quirk** (ritual, absurd heirloom, old-world vibe).

### **Prestige / Mythic Cool**
- Veil: high ritual elegance  
- Neon: **absurd collision royalty** (prestige embraces mashup)  
- Loom: calm-ritual austerity with one prismatic symbol  
**Cool source:** cultural gravity, not gadgets.

---

## 5) Visual “Awe Moments” (allowed, bounded)
These are your big-ticket scenery beats that *don’t* become sci-fi:

1) **AR-enabled rail dining car** in Clear/Haze  
   - shared ambiance packs  
   - window skins shifting to personal vistas  
   - then a Squall wipe → raw reality snaps in  
   **Awe source:** juxtaposition.

2) **Neon bloom storm over a city skyline**  
   - saturated tear-light + sound-warp  
   - crowds reacting like weather animals  
   **Awe source:** civic emotion rendered physical.

3) **Loom prismatic front rolling over corridor convoys**  
   - silhouettes, breathing horizon, geometric air  
   **Awe source:** survival under beauty-terror.

4) **Post-mending Echo Node nights**  
   - soft mandala shimmer spots, warm halos  
   **Awe source:** gentleness after apocalypse.

---

## 6) Fast “scene palette picker” (for your beats)
When you set a scene, choose:

1) **Trilogy climate** (Veil / Neon / Loom / Post)  
2) **City dialect** (one of the anchor lanes)  
3) **Weather rung modifier** (if any)  
4) **Tier coolness lane** (common / middle / luxury / prestige)  
5) **One resonance overlay color family** (Hybrid VFX spectrum) **only if pressure or emotion peaks**

That combo gives you instant consistency.

---

If you want to keep going, the natural companion system is **Standard Kit / Equipment Ecology** — because now we know what things *look like*, we decide what objects people reliably have in those looks.

# STANDARD KIT / EQUIPMENT ECOLOGY v1 (Track-1 Context)

## Purpose
Define what people **normally carry / rely on** across Veil → Neon → Loom, by tier and role, so scenes feel lived-in and logically constrained.

This is **not** weapons (already done) and **not** MT gear (already done).  
This is the **everyday survival + travel + repair + comms-fallback layer**.

---

## 0) Master rules (always on)
1) **Kits shrink under pressure.**  
   Veil = convenience. Neon = preparedness. Loom = survival essentials only.

2) **Every kit has analog fallback.**  
   If AR dies, people still move, trade, heal, and signal.

3) **Coolness comes from *use*, not novelty.**  
   One signature hack tells the story.

4) **Tier changes *finish*, not function.**  
   Everyone needs water, light, warmth, navigation, proof.

---

## 1) Universal “Five-Pocket” Kit  
Most adults have **some form of these**, even if poor.

1) **Light**
   - Veil: phone light, slim torch  
   - Neon: crank/hand torch, chem-glow sticks  
   - Loom: storm-rated lantern nub + spare cells

2) **Water / salts**
   - Veil: bottle  
   - Neon: bottle + purification tabs  
   - Loom: collapsible canteen + electrolyte packet

3) **Patch / repair**
   - Veil: tape, multitool  
   - Neon: heavier tape, stitch kit, small wire  
   - Loom: patch squares, cord, rivet clip

4) **Navigation fallback**
   - Veil: AR maps / phone GPS  
   - Neon: printed corridor cards + chime-nav pips  
   - Loom: laminated paper route + compass bead

5) **Proof / identity**
   - Veil: digital ID  
   - Neon: printed passes + stamped glyphs  
   - Loom: **role band** + pocket witness card (name/affiliation/next-of-kin)

---

## 2) Era kits (baseline civilian)

### **Veil Era baseline**
**Function vibe:** convenience with subtle preparedness.
- Phone + AR frames  
- Slim power bank  
- Basic first-aid strip (bandage, antiseptic wipe)  
- Earbuds / tiny mic (holochat common)  
- City transit card  
- Small umbrella / coat shell  
- Minimal patch kit  
- Little to no food carry unless traveling

**What feels “weird” if missing:** phone, some ID, everyday transit access.

---

### **Neon Era baseline**
**Function vibe:** urban survival kit is normalized.
- AR frames (often scuffed) + **audio fallback buds**  
- Power slab or scavenged battery pack  
- **Chime-nav key** (simple tone compass for corridors)  
- Printed corridor card / knot list  
- Light: crank torch + 1–2 glow sticks  
- Small food block (jerky, nut bar, ration wafer)  
- Water tabs  
- Patch kit: tape + stitch + wire  
- Basic med: tourniquet strip, burn gel, anti-nausea tabs  
- **Sticker stack / glyph chalk** (mark safe/unsafe)

**What feels “weird” if missing:** light, water tabs, some analog map.

---

### **Loom Era baseline**
**Function vibe:** collapse survival, everything redundant.
- AR gear often absent or dead; if present it’s audio-only.  
- **Analog route slab** (laminated card, folded map, corridor glyphs)  
- Compass bead or magnet-thread  
- **Storm lantern nub** + spare cells  
- Canteen + salts  
- 2–3 food blocks minimum  
- Repair roll: patch squares + cord + rivet clips  
- Med roll: clot pads, tourniquet, pain tabs, anti-shock sugar gel  
- **Role/affiliation band visible**  
- Simple shelter tool: foil blanket or waxed tarp square  
- Personal ritual token (grounding object)

**What feels “weird” if missing:** water, light, map, visible role band.

---

## 3) Tier gradients (how kits look, not what they do)

### **Common / DIY**
- Function-first, mismatched parts  
- Scavenged batteries, shoelace cord, patched pouches  
- Hand-written corridor notes taped inside clothing  
- Light sources ugly but reliable  
**Signature cool:** one clever hack (e.g., lantern made from jar + coil).

### **Middle / Prepared**
- Modular pouches, clean labeling  
- Better med and battery redundancy  
- Quiet colorway, calm finish  
**Signature cool:** competence as style.

### **Luxury / Sanctuary**
- Same functions, higher finish  
- Hidden weight distribution, silent closures  
- Better comfort: heat liners, good boots  
- Storage feels elegant and boring on purpose  
**Signature cool:** one human quirk (old rail pin, absurd heirloom tool).

### **Prestige**
- Veil: ritualized travel kits (leather cases, heirloom optics)  
- Neon: prestige embraces mashup (old-world case + resopunk mods)  
- Loom: austere calm kits, almost monastic  
**Signature cool:** cultural gravity.

---

## 4) Role-based kit overlays  
These sit *on top of* the baseline era kit.

### **Corridor / Convoy travelers**
- Pod whistle / chime  
- 1 extra water unit  
- Spare sock/liner  
- Simple dust mask or scarf  
- “Buddy cord” (to stay linked in Gale/Landfall)

### **Rail / Liner crew**
- **Conductor/quiet-car glyph set**  
- Hand lantern baton  
- Car-level analog roster cards  
- Emergency food/water cache keys  
- Calm-protocol cue cards (short phrases)

### **Med / Triage**
- Expanded clot kit  
- Burn wraps  
- Fluids salts  
- Quick-tag chalk  
- Simple sedation / panic-grounding aids  
- “Do not move” bands for fractures

### **Concord field**
- Hardened comms slab (audio-first)  
- Role bands + checksum seals  
- Weather meter bead  
- Extra battery + med redundancy  
- Portable shade/cover square  
- Quiet-signal chimes for squads

### **Filament elders / civic anchors**
- Grounding tokens  
- Small shared-aid kit  
- Chalk/glyph kit  
- Food share pouch  
- Low-tech musical/noise tool (to regulate crowds)

---

## 5) Infrastructure objects that recur in scenes
These aren’t carried on bodies, but they’re *everywhere* enough to feel normal.

### **Neon**
- Charging bars at Knots  
- Corridor boards with layered stickers  
- Public “quiet corners” built for AR-off rituals  
- Patch markets (cloth, cord, batteries, glow sticks)

### **Loom**
- Ration boards  
- Embedded lantern hooks on walls/vehicles  
- Salvage drop bins  
- Whisper libraries (paper maps + witness ledgers)  
- Corridor gates with visible role-check stations

---

## 6) Quick “scene legitimacy” test
If a scene involves travel or crowd survival, make sure you can answer in one sentence:

1) **Where’s their light?**  
2) **How are they drinking safely?**  
3) **What’s their analog fallback?**  
4) **How do they prove identity/role?**  
5) **What’s the one signature hack that says who they are?**

If you can answer those, your kit ecology is doing its job.

---

# RESONANCE WEATHER OPERATIONS ADDENDUM v1 (Track-1 Context)

## Purpose
Show that people **live with resonance weather** the way we live with storms:  
warnings, folk practices, corridor rules, prep rituals, and predictable mistakes.

This makes weather feel **socially real**, not just visually cool.

---

## 0) Master ops rules (always on)
1) **Two forecast streams always coexist:**  
   - **Official** (Dominions/Technarch/municipal)  
   - **Folk** (corridor watchers, elders, MT, Knot boards)

2) **Trust shifts by era:**  
   Veil trusts officials → Neon trusts folk/MT → Loom trusts corridors/Concord.

3) **Ops are rung-triggered.**  
   Every city/vehicle/shelter has simple rung scripts.

4) **Etiquette is safety tech.**  
   Calm rules are a *real survival system* in Squall+.

---

## 1) Forecast Ecosystem

### Veil Era (Books 1–3)
**Official**
- City alerts, weather-style maps, “anomaly advisories.”
- Most people treat them like quirky infrastructure warnings.

**Folk**
- Small: bar whispers, local elders, niche MT threads.
- First “Ghostwave watchers” form here.

**Common drift**
- People **underreact** to Haze/Squall because life is still normal.

---

### Neon Era (Books 4–6)
**Official**
- Mixed credibility; some cities downplay for stability.
- Technarch releases “calibration advisories” that feel overly clinical.

**Folk**
- Dominant: corridor boards + Knot runners + MT receipts.
- “Blue-line” maps go viral and mutate by neighborhood.

**Common drift**
- People **overreact** sometimes (panic surges),  
  but they also **survive more** because they share lived rules.

---

### Loom Era (Books 7–9)
**Official**
- Fragmented or gone.

**Concord + Folk**
- “Route truth” becomes sacred.
- Forecasting is localized, human, and role-based.

**Common drift**
- People trust the **quietest, most grounded voice**.

---

## 2) Warning Systems (what you see / hear)

### Official markers (Veil → early Neon)
- Map overlays, siren/chime advisories, transit slowdowns.
- Coded phrasing like “pressure front,” “AR instability expected.”

### Folk markers (Neon → Loom)
- **Knot boards**: layered stickers + chalk glyphs with timestamps.  
- Handwritten “last safe window” notes.  
- Corridor runners shouting call-and-response warnings.  
- MT clip bursts used as “proof of sky behavior.”

### Universal non-tech markers (all eras)
- **Streetlight flicker cadence**
- **Public bell/hum lines**
- **Door/rail glyphs updated daily**
- **Human watchers on rooftops / platforms** in Neon/Loom

---

## 3) Rung Scripts (what communities *do*)

### Clear
- Normal operations.
- Transit runs full schedules.

### Haze
**City / transit**
- “Soft alert” posted.  
- Crew quietly checks batteries + doors.

**People**
- Start carrying light + water tabs.
- First folk jokes: “air’s thin today.”

---

### Squall
**City**
- Corridors activate officially or by habit.
- Nonessential events fade out.

**Transit**
- Trains/liners switch to **safety pacing**.
- Dining/lounge AR packs prepped for wipe.

**Shelters / Knots**
- Charging rotations posted.
- Calm etiquette rules go up at entrances.

**People**
- Pod discipline begins.
- Kids/elders kept indoors.
- Everyone assumes glass is lying soon.

---

### Gale
**City**
- Curfews in Veil/early Neon; in Loom this is “stay close to Knots.”
- Markets shrink into protected clusters.

**Transit**
- Long-haul stops unless corridor-verified.
- Quiet-car protocol enforced.

**Shelters**
- Doors lock to role bands only.
- **Audio-first comms** only.

**People**
- Movement by **human signs + touch + buddy cords**.
- Emotional arguments treated as hazards.

---

### Landfall
**City**
- Everything becomes survival-local.  
- Past a point, only triage + evacuation matter.

**Transit**
- Stops unless life-or-death.
- Crews convert cars into shelter pods.

**Shelters**
- Strictest ration + calm rule enforcement.
- Witness/role check at threshold.

**People**
- Close to ground, close to each other.
- Recording becomes sacred proof.

---

## 4) Calm Etiquette (safety social contract)
**These rules show up on trains, Knots, convoy pods, and shelters.**  
They let you show discipline without exposition.

1) **Speak simple.**
2) **Confirm-back.**
3) **Move in pods.**
4) **Follow role bands.**
5) **No shouting unless it’s a warning.**
6) **Humor is allowed if it steadies.**
7) **No debating the sky.**

**Violation consequences (Neon/Loom):**
- pod separation  
- panic contagion  
- Concord intervention  
- ejection from safe zones

---

## 5) Shelter / Knot Operations
### Veil
- Shelters exist mostly as “anomaly centers” or civic stations.
- People go reluctantly.

### Neon
- Knots become **social + power + route truth hubs.**
- Staffing mix: elders, med anchors, ex-transit, Concord-adjacent.
- **Charging and ration rotations** are the heart.

### Loom
- Knots become survival governments.
- Rules are strict but emotionally humane.
- **Witness ledgers** and role checks prevent collapse.

---

## 6) Corridor Operations (travel reality)
### Blue-lines
“Safe-ish routes” that:
- avoid shard scars  
- follow stable emotional basins  
- rely on lived observation

**Maintenance:**
- updated daily by watchers and runners  
- marked physically with Blue Thread glyphs  
- verified by Concord in Loom

### Travel windows
People travel in **low-pressure lulls**, not by clock.  
Runners call windows like tides:
- “two hours clear to the east”  
- “front rolling over by dusk”

---

## 7) Common mistakes (good for plot)
### Veil mistakes
- assuming advisories are overblown
- treating Haze/Squall as aesthetic weirdness
- relying on AR/maps too long

### Neon mistakes
- chasing storms for rumor/clicks
- misreading folk boards
- splitting pods to “save time”
- trying to livestream in Squall+, draining everything

### Loom mistakes
- hoarding power/food (causes social fracture)
- ignoring calm protocol during grief spikes
- trusting old official maps over lived route truth

These mistakes are **earned stakes generators**.

---

## 8) Cultural variation (small but punchy)
- **NOLA:** music-as-stabilizer, parade signaling, joy rituals turn fear into cohesion.  
- **Vienna:** quiet discipline; shelters feel like libraries; shame spikes cause sudden breaks.  
- **Singapore:** protocol-heavy ops; grids until they fail; strong role-band legitimacy.  
- **Reykjavik/Arctic:** fewer fronts, clearer warnings; communal wool rituals, geothermal safe-havens.  
- **Marrakesh:** market councils as forecast hubs; colorful folk boards; desert wind metaphors.  
- **Swamp corridors:** boat/airship etiquette, hush rules, still-pocket navigation.

---

## 9) Reader-intuitive cue set (no jargon)
To cue ops in prose, you only need **one line** like:
- “Blue Thread marks were fresh on the doors.”  
- “The dining car was already in audio-only mode.”  
- “Quiet-car rules went up without anyone being told.”  
- “A runner shouted the travel window down the platform.”  
- “Everyone clipped buddy cords before stepping outside.”

Those lines *immediately tell the reader what rung they’re in*.

---

- Loom ration + rotation logic (power, food, med priority, ammo rarity)

# LIGHT ECONOMY / BARTER / RESOURCE FLOW v1 (Track-1 Context)

## Purpose
Define what becomes valuable, how people trade, and who gains legitimacy **as the Veil collapses**—so beats never feel hand-wavey.

Scope is **tight**: resource logic, barter norms, prestige travel economics, and a few flavor “prices.”  
No macroeconomics, no currency charts.

---

## 0) Master economy rules (always on)
1) **Scarcity follows weather + infrastructure failure.**  
   The more Squall/Gale/Landfall, the more the economy shifts from money → survival goods.

2) **Power is the first true currency.**  
   Because it drives comms, nav, triage, and trust.

3) **Information is a traded good, not free.**  
   Route truth, storm windows, and Knot safety ratings are economic assets.

4) **Legitimacy = who can distribute fairly under pressure.**  
   That’s how Knots and Concord gain authority without politics exposition.

---

## 1) Veil Era Economy (Books 1–3)
**Baseline:** normal late-modern economy with subtle friction.
- Money works. Markets work. Travel is mostly consumer-logic.
- Power feels invisible/expected.
- Resonance disruptions are rare, localized, and treated like infrastructure anomalies.

**Early shift signals (late Veil):**
- **Micro-markets** emerge around Shard rumor zones:  
  batteries, film/analog recorders, “stabilizer charms” (folk).
- Officials quietly **deny/limit** anomaly impact to protect stability.

**Narrative use:**  
Veil economy is your “control group.” When it breaks, readers feel the stakes.

---

## 2) Neon Era Economy (Books 4–6)
**Core shift:** money still exists, but **barter layers over it** wherever Squall+ is common or trust is low.

### Neon Barter Pillars (what everyone wants)
1) **Power/Charging**
   - battery slabs  
   - charging time  
   - generator fuel  
   - solar/crank parts

2) **Water + Purification**
   - tabs/filters  
   - clean refill access

3) **Med / Calm**
   - clot pads, burn gel, anti-nausea  
   - panic-grounding pills/ritual tools  
   - bandages become tradable

4) **Route Truth**
   - Blue-line updates  
   - Knot ratings  
   - travel windows  
   - safe-pod introductions

5) **Repair Materials**
   - patch cloth  
   - cord  
   - rivets  
   - glow sticks / lantern nubs

### How trade *feels*
- **Charging bars** at Knots become barter altars.  
- People pay for:
  - a **slot**  
  - a **safe outlet**  
  - a **verified window** to move  
- MT receipts often function as currency-adjacent proof.

### Prestige travel economics (Neon)
Trains, liners, airships become **sanctuary economies**:
- They sell **safety, calm protocol, and stable charging** more than speed.
- Ticketing becomes part money, part **credential/trust network**.
- Luxury is not always coolest; **prestige is safety + culture**.

### Neon “folk price” flavor (loose norms)
- 1 hour clean charging ≈ 1 food block **or** 1 verified route note.  
- 1 purified water fill ≈ small repair bundle or two glow sticks.  
- A *fresh* corridor window tip may cost a med strip or full charging slot.

**Narrative use:**  
Neon economy is where ingenuity shines; barter creates instant social tension.

---

## 3) Loom Era Economy (Books 7–9)
**Core shift:** economy becomes **rotations + ration boards + legitimacy systems**.  
Money is background or irrelevant outside sanctuaries.

### Loom Scarcity Pillars (order of reality)
1) **Power**
   - lantern cells  
   - generator fuel  
   - rotation slots  
   - comms priority access

2) **Food**
   - ration blocks  
   - corridor farming caches  
   - barter for calories

3) **Water**
   - safe sources guarded  
   - purification capacity counts

4) **Med**
   - clot/sedation/pain management  
   - trauma care becomes **community-governed**

5) **Ammunition / functional weapons parts**
   - bullets become rare  
   - close-tool repairs matter more  
   - factions hoard → fractures

6) **Route Truth / Escort**
   - safe windows  
   - pod introductions  
   - Concord escort slots

### Loom distribution mechanics
- **Ration boards** are visible in every Knot.  
- **Rotations** are sacred:
  - charging  
  - watch shifts  
  - med triage priority  
- “Fairness” is survival tech.

### Who runs Knots in Loom?
Whoever can reliably manage:
- power allocation  
- food rationing  
- med triage  
- route truth verification  
Often a coalition:
- elders + med anchors + corridor runners  
- Concord-adjacent stabilizers  
- sometimes ex-transit crew (order mindset)

**Legitimacy test:**  
If a leader breaks rotation fairness, the Knot fractures.

### Loom prestige travel economics
Sanctuary travel continues but becomes:
- **credential-based** (role bands, checksum trust)  
- **resource-backed** (you contribute fuel/med/escort skill)  
- tickets become **mutual survival contracts**, not purchases.

### Loom “folk price” flavor
- One lantern cell ≈ one full med strip **or** two food blocks.  
- A verified escort through Gale ≈ a night of rations **plus** public credit on the board.  
- A full safe window across a scar can cost **everything** you’re carrying.

**Narrative use:**  
Loom economy is how you make collapse feel real without exposition.

---

## 4) Antagonist & faction economic behaviors
Economy shapes threat style.

### Dominions
- Leverage **legacy stockpiles + credential gatekeeping.**
- Weaponize scarcity to enforce obedience.

### Technarch Hardliners
- Control **power + grids + damping rigs.**
- When tech fails, they lose legitimacy fast.

### Choirless
- Trade in **calm-as-safety.**
- Sanctuaries that require emotional suppression as “payment.”

### Brightbreak / Militias
- Run on **loot economy + fear taxation.**
- Hoarding ammo/power fractures communities.

### Filament networks
- **Mutual aid economics.**
- Trade in **ritual grounding, safe pods, and trust.**
- Their currency is belonging.

---

## 5) Reader-intuitive cues (no jargon)
You can show economy with quick concrete lines:

- “The charging board was already full for the night.”  
- “She traded a lantern cell for two purification tabs.”  
- “Blue-line updates cost a meal now.”  
- “Rations were posted in chalk by the door.”  
- “Prestige tickets weren’t bought — they were earned.”

Those lines instantly communicate the era’s economy.

---

## 6) Quick economy sanity check for beats
For any travel/riot/siege beat, answer:

1) **What’s scarce here right now?**  
2) **Who controls distribution?**  
3) **What’s the traded good in this scene?** *(power/food/med/route/repair)*  
4) **What does a fair system look like — and who’s breaking it?**

If you know those, the beat will feel inevitable.

# CIVIC / SOCIAL ORDER UNDER COLLAPSE v1 (Track-1 Context)

## Purpose
Define how **ordinary governance, community order, and legitimacy** morph from Veil → Neon → Loom, so crowd scenes, riots, evacuations, and alliances feel coherent.

This is not civics class. It’s **lived order** under pressure.

---

## 0) Master social-order rules (always on)
1) **Legitimacy comes from distribution.**  
   Whoever can fairly allocate **power, food, med, and route truth** becomes “the government.”

2) **Order shrinks to the human scale.**  
   Neighborhoods → corridors → Knots → convoys.

3) **Every era has two orders running in parallel:**  
   - **Formal order** (institutions, uniforms, laws)  
   - **Folk order** (elders, runners, rituals, boards)

4) **Calm etiquette is a civic technology.**  
   Not vibes — *survival infrastructure*.

5) **Violence rises when distribution fails.**  
   Your low-violence saga stays grounded because the system incentivizes de-escalation where possible.

---

## 1) Veil Era Social Order (Books 1–3)
**Baseline:** modern civic structure with subtle friction.

### What runs the world
- Governments, police, courts, transit authorities, hospitals.  
- Dominions and Technarch influence behind the scenes.

### How order feels
- Rules are impersonal, bureaucratic, slow.  
- People expect systems to work.

### Early fracture signs (late Veil)
- “Anomaly response teams” appear, quiet and vague.  
- Officials downplay pressure to preserve calm.  
- Folk watchers begin to form **micro-trust networks** (bars, covens, archivists, MT followers).

**Narrative use:**  
Veil order is dependable *until it isn’t*. That contrast is your emotional fuse.

---

## 2) Neon Era Social Order (Books 4–6)
**Core shift:** formal order is still alive, but **folk order becomes equally real**.

### The three-tier civic stack
1) **Institutional Cities**
   - still have mayors, police, transit, Technarch grids  
   - legitimacy erodes with every cover-up or failure  
2) **Corridors**
   - semi-official safe routes  
   - maintained by watchers + runners; sometimes tolerated by officials  
3) **Knots**
   - hubs of charging, shelter, barter, route truth  
   - effectively **neighborhood governments**

### Who runs a Knot (Neon)
Usually a coalition:
- Filament elders / civic anchors  
- Med triage lead  
- Corridor runner captain  
- Sometimes ex-transit or ex-utility manager  
- Sometimes Concord-adjacent stabilizer (quietly)

**Legitimacy test:**  
People follow whoever **keeps rotations fair**.

### How order feels
- Mixed. One street acts normal, next street is corridor law.  
- Rules are visible: boards, glyphs, role cues.

### Enforcement style (Neon)
- **Soft first, hard when needed.**  
- Calm etiquette enforced socially; physical enforcement is last resort.  
- “Pod discipline” becomes the civic unit of safety.

### Failure mode (Neon)
- If grids fail and ration/charge fairness breaks:
  - panic spirals  
  - militia taxation  
  - Choirless “calm sanctuaries” recruit  
  - Brightbreak rises  
  - institutional police crack down → worsen volatility

**Narrative use:**  
Neon is the era of *competing legitimacies*. Great for tension without needing big politics.

---

## 3) Loom Era Social Order (Books 7–9)
**Core shift:** order becomes **corridor-scale survival governance**.

### The Loom civic stack
1) **Knots as City-States**
   - ration boards  
   - charging rotations  
   - med triage councils  
   - witness ledgers  
   - role-band checks  
2) **Convoys / Pods**
   - mobile civic units  
   - their own etiquette, leaders, med rules  
3) **Concord**
   - not a government, but a **stabilizing federation**
   - verifies route truth, enforces calm protocols in crises, supports Knots

### Loom leadership archetypes
- **The Quartermaster** (power + food fairness)  
- **The Triage Lead** (med legitimacy)  
- **The Runner Captain** (route truth)  
- **The Elder Anchor** (ritual calm + moral trust)  
- **The Concord Liaison** (coordination + defense)

Often these are **different people**, balancing each other.

### How order feels
- Smaller, more intimate, more human.  
- Rules are blunt and visible; everyone knows the stakes.

### Enforcement style (Loom)
- **Etiquette is primary enforcement.**
- Visible role bands decide privileges:
  - med priority  
  - charging priority  
  - convoy decisions  
- Violence is a failure state; communities avoid it unless survival requires it.

### Loom failure mode
- Hoarding power/food/med breaks legitimacy instantly.  
- Militia fear-taxation makes people flee to better Knots.  
- Emotional contagion during Gale/Landfall can topple any order *fast*.

**Narrative use:**  
Loom order gives you clean stakes for siege and survival without needing new villains.

---

## 4) How factions interact with civic order

### Dominions
- Try to **re-impose hierarchical order** via stockpiles and credential control.  
- Their legitimacy decays wherever corridors/Knots outperform them.

### Technarch Hardliners
- Govern by grids, surveillance, damping rigs.  
- Lose authority when tech fails or people stop believing.

### Choirless
- Offer “calm sanctuaries” as civic order.  
- Their order is emotionally coercive; works short-term, collapses long-term.

### Brightbreak / militias
- Build order through fear taxation + loot.  
- Short-lived but dangerous where Knots fracture.

### Filament networks
- Mutual aid + ritual calm governance.  
- Not utopian; just *functional human order*.

### Concord
- Stabilizer federation.  
- Gains legitimacy by:
  - escorting corridors  
  - sharing resources fairly  
  - refusing to be the new Dominion

---

## 5) Civic ritual systems (the social “OS”)
These are repeatable behaviors that keep order alive.

### Veil rituals
- Mostly invisible: institutional schedules, normal commute patterns.

### Neon rituals
- charging rotations  
- corridor windows called like tides  
- Knot board updates morning/evening  
- quiet corners / AR-off grounding spaces  
- shared meal “calm tables”

### Loom rituals
- ration posting at dawn  
- role-band checks at thresholds  
- buddy-cord clipping before travel  
- grief-processing circles to stop contagion spirals  
- witness ledger sign-ins after storms

**Rule:** rituals are not flavor; they are **anti-panic tech**.

---

## 6) “Show-don’t-tell” cues for order
One line in a scene can establish the current civic state:

- “She checked the ration board before she spoke.”  
- “Quiet-car rules were up; nobody questioned it.”  
- “The runner stamped the Blue Thread mark on his wrist.”  
- “Role bands decided who got the outlets first.”  
- “The old police station was a Knot now.”  
- “Concord didn’t give orders—just updates and escort windows.”

---

## 7) Sanity check for any crowd/riot/evac beat
Answer these four quickly:

1) **What’s scarce here right now?**  
2) **Who is distributing it?**  
3) **What etiquette or ritual is keeping this from breaking?**  
4) **What happens if that ritual fails?**

If you know those, your social order is airtight.

# RESOPUNK INFRASTRUCTURE & EVERYDAY TECH v1 (Track-1 Context)

## Purpose
Define the **ambient tech + patched infrastructure** people live inside across Veil → Neon → Loom → Post-Mending.

This is not AR/Holo (already canon).  
This is what makes cities, vehicles, shelters, and homes *feel real* under pressure.

---

## 0) Master infra rules (always on)
1) **Tech is human-made, janky, and local.**  
   No magic, no seamless utopia.

2) **Resopunk = hybrid analog/digital built to survive interference.**  
   If it can’t survive Squall, it gets replaced or downgraded.

3) **Visibility increases with collapse.**  
   Veil hides infrastructure → Neon exposes ribs → Loom is all patchwork → Post-mending repairs settle.

4) **Failure is graceful and predictable.**  
   Same downshift ladder as comms: rich → simplified → low-res → audio → analog.

---

## 1) Veil Era Infrastructure (Books 1–3)
**Vibe:** nearly-modern, slightly anachronistic drag.

### Power
- Stable grid, mostly invisible.  
- Backup generators exist but feel like normal “emergency systems.”

### Lighting
- Modern street/hospital lighting.  
- **Occasional** lantern-glow artifacts at shard sites (subtle uncanny).

### Transit infrastructure
- Airports, highways, trains normal.  
- Long-distance trains are **prestige nostalgia** more than necessity.

### Civilian devices
- Phones, AR frames, normal household appliances.  
- Holochat hubs in offices/public buildings.

**Reader feel:** “Our world, mostly OK.”

---

## 2) Neon Era Infrastructure (Books 4–6)
**Vibe:** patched modernity + visible survival tech; resopunk emerges.

### Power
- **Power islands** + rolling brownouts.  
- Neighborhood microgrids become common.  
- Charging bars at Knots are civic centers.

**What survives best:**  
simple solar mats, crank gens, fuel-patch generators.

### Lighting
- Light becomes **functional signaling**:
  - corridor lamps change cadence by rung  
  - Knot entrances glow in safe colors  
- Neon tear-light distortions show stress.

### Transit infrastructure
- **Rail rises**:
  - hardened analog control backups  
  - “quiet cars” pre-built for AR-off protocols  
- Roads degrade where off-grid; maintenance becomes local barter economy.

### Civilian devices (resopunk layer)
- **Chime-nav keys** (audio direction cues)  
- **Rugged slabs** replacing fragile phones  
- **Analog-assist cookers** (mechanical dials with digital readouts)  
- **Paper-screen hybrids** (e-ink + physical switches)  
- “Resonance ribs” visible on many devices: conductive threads / glow lines that *don’t transmit resonance* but survive interference.

### Public space mods
- Quiet corners (AR-off ritual spaces)  
- Patch markets selling batteries cloth cord glow sticks  
- Corridor boards everywhere.

**Reader feel:** “Beautiful improvisation inside a cracking world.”

---

## 3) Loom Era Infrastructure (Books 7–9)
**Vibe:** collapse tech: bare-bones, analog-first, survival patchwork.

### Power
- Grid mostly dead.  
- Microgeneration everywhere:
  - hand cranks  
  - scavenged solar  
  - fuel patch generators  
- **Rotations** govern all charging.

### Lighting
- **Lantern ecology**:
  - hooks built into walls/vehicles  
  - community lantern caches  
- Lighting becomes a civic language:
  - calm zones = warm steady  
  - danger fronts = flicker cadence  
- Prismatic weather can drown light; silhouettes dominate.

### Transit infrastructure
- **Corridors are physical**:
  - marked gates  
  - role checks  
  - buddy-cord stations  
- Vehicles are survival rigs:
  - patched engines  
  - analog dashboards  
  - “storm tires”  
- Trains/liners/airships become moving cities with ration/triage boards onboard.

### Civilian/home devices
Everything is designed to work with **no network**:
- hand-pump water purifiers  
- crank radios (audio-first comms)  
- mechanical heaters/cooktops with fuel efficiency hacks  
- repair benches in shelters  
- paper libraries + witness ledgers

**What dies first:**  
- complex sensors  
- high-res screens  
- cloud-dependent anything

**Reader feel:** “The world runs on hands, rituals, and fairness.”

---

## 4) Post-Mending Infrastructure (Epilogue / future)
**Vibe:** repaired stability, not utopia.

### Power
- Grid returns gently.  
- Microgeneration remains a cultural habit.

### Lighting
- Calmer, more natural; Echo Nodes provide faint harmonic glow.  
- Less signaling needed, but rituals persist.

### Devices
- AR stabilizes again, but resopunk habits remain:
  - physical switches valued  
  - audio fallback still standard  
- Communities keep some corridor boards as memory/tradition.

**Reader feel:** “We learned, then rebuilt.”

---

## 5) Infrastructure & Faction Dialects

### Dominions
- High finish, old-world infrastructure.
- Prefer hidden systems until forced into resopunk exposure.

### Technarch Hardliners
- Grid-first ideology.
- Device-heavy, brittle advantage.
- Their infrastructure “looks inevitable” right up until it fails.

### Choirless
- Minimalist, sound-damp, ritual-sync tech.
- Use calm-bubble tools, low-sensory lighting.

### Filaments / civic anchors
- Mutual-aid infra:
  - community charging circles  
  - repair benches  
  - musical grounding tools  
- Their tech looks handmade but *works*.

### Concord
- Hardened, field-rated systems:
  - audio-first comms slabs  
  - portable microgrids  
  - triage kits  
  - corridor verification tools  
- Their infra is defined by **reliability under Gale/Landfall**.

---

## 6) Recurrent infra set pieces (high-ROI scenery)
These are your “effortless cool” props that repeat without explanation:

1) **Charging bars** with posted rotations.  
2) **Corridor gates** with Blue Thread marks and role checks.  
3) **Quiet corners** (AR-off ritual spaces).  
4) **Repair markets / benches** in Knots.  
5) **Lantern hook lines** along streets, trains, and shelters.  
6) **E-ink + mechanical dial devices** everywhere.  
7) **Analog control rooms** on trains/liners/airships.  
8) **Witness ledgers** as civic memory tech.

---

## 7) “Show-don’t-tell” infra cues
To render resopunk instantly:

- “Every device had a real switch again.”  
- “The corridor gate hummed only in audio.”  
- “Lantern hooks ran down the platform like ribs.”  
- “She checked the e-ink slab, then the paper map taped beneath it.”  
- “The repair bench was the busiest place in the Knot.”  
- “The grid was a rumor; the crank was real.”

---

## 8) Sanity check for any environment beat
Answer in one sentence:

1) **What’s powering this place right now?**  
2) **What lighting language is active?**  
3) **What’s the analog fallback if AR dies?**  
4) **What’s the visible patch that tells us the era?**

If you can answer those, the setting is coherent.

# 1) MATERIALS / FABRICS / MAKE-DO TEXTURE v1 (Region + Era)

## Purpose
Give you a **material language** that:
- makes cultures feel distinct
- tracks Veil → Neon → Loom drift
- reinforces resopunk + weather reality
- supports “cool without sci-fi”

This is *not* a fashion catalog. It’s the **material ecosystem** beneath fashion.

---

## Master rules
1) **Materials are survival logic first, style second.**  
2) **Every era has a dominant wear-problem.**  
   - Veil: identity + class texture  
   - Neon: interference + repair  
   - Loom: weather + scarcity  
3) **Regional flavor = climate + craft tradition + collapse adaptation.**  
4) **Resonance doesn’t change materials directly** — it changes *how people choose* and *what survives.*

---

## Era material gradients (global)

### **Veil (Books 1–3): “Modern with old-world drag”**
**Dominant problem:** status/identity, not survival.  
- **Everyday:** cotton, denim, leather, wool blends, synthetics in normal proportions.  
- **Prestige:** tailoring fabrics, fine leathers, silk accents, linen in heat zones.  
- **Texture vibe:** clean surfaces; wear reads as character choice, not necessity.  
- **Coolness lane:** “quiet inevitability,” not patchwork showiness.

**Visual shorthand:** well-made + slightly anachronistic (old brass, real buttons, nice seams).

---

### **Neon (Books 4–6): “Resopunk repair culture”**
**Dominant problem:** interference + sudden travel risk.  
- **Favorite survivors:**  
  - **reinforced knits** (stretch + durability)  
  - **waxed cotton** (weather + grime resistance)  
  - **ripstop synthetics** (cheap, light, patchable)  
  - **real leather** (lasts, barters well)  
- **Signature Neon textures:**  
  - visible stitching  
  - patched panels  
  - “resonance ribs” (conductive trim lines used for durability/grounding *not* magic)  
  - scuffed acrylic/metal accessories  
- **Coolness lane:** mashup ingenuity, visible fixes.

**Visual shorthand:** repaired seams are badges; clothes have “history.”

---

### **Loom (Books 7–9): “Storm-rated survival wear”**
**Dominant problem:** resonance fronts + scarcity + corridor living.  
- **Core fabrics:**  
  - **heavy waxed canvas** (storm/brush armor)  
  - **quilted insulation layers** (warmth, impact damping)  
  - **salvaged technical synthetics** (from uniforms/vehicles)  
  - **rope/cord integration** (buddy-cords, tie-downs)  
  - **rubberized panels** (boots, gloves, storm trims)  
- **Material logic:**  
  - everything is dual-use (coat can become shelter square, belt becomes cord)  
  - repairs are chunky and fast  
  - pockets become architecture  
- **Coolness lane:** calm ritual austerity + one resonant symbol.

**Visual shorthand:** stormwear looks like people expect to *move through danger today.*

---

### **Post-Mending: “Repaired craft calm”**
**Dominant problem:** none urgent — culture reasserts.  
- **Materials soften again:** cleaner weaves, fewer armor panels.  
- **Resopunk habits persist:** physical toggles, durable stitching, modularity.  
- **Coolness lane:** gentleness + craftsmanship + memory.

**Visual shorthand:** patched things remain, but now they’re intentional heirlooms.

---

## Regional material dialects (anchor lanes + beyond)

### **New Orleans / Gulf Humidity**
**Climate problem:** wet heat + mold + sweat.  
- breathable cotton/linen  
- **oil-treated leather** (humidity-proof)  
- brass, beads, enamel accessories  
- rubber-soled boots for flood streets  
- in Neon/Loom: **light wax coats and wrap-ponchos** with bright linings  
**Flavor:** materials *look like music feels* — warm, resilient, tactile.

---

### **Vienna / Central Gothic Old World**
**Climate problem:** cold damp + formal legacy.  
- wool, felt, velvet remnants  
- polished leather, dark metals  
- archival paper textiles (tags, seals, ribbons)  
- in Neon: tailored garments repaired with **visible but precise stitching**  
- in Loom: **structured coats** surviving as authority relics  
**Flavor:** restraint, weight, clean lines, subsidized elegance breaking into repairs.

---

### **Singapore / Vertical Precision Tropics**
**Climate problem:** heat + rain + high tech density.  
- **technical synthetics** that breathe  
- modular panels, magnetic closures  
- clean composites, carbon-fiber trim in prestige  
- in Neon: grids and seams get “ribbed” with conductive trim for durability  
- in Loom: modular kits re-used as clothing layers  
**Flavor:** engineered quietness, high function, minimal waste.

---

### **Reykjavik / Arctic & High-Latitude**
**Climate problem:** wind + cold + ice.  
- wool layers, shearling, oilskin  
- geothermal-steam wear (that looks slightly damp-clean)  
- basalt/metal fasteners  
- Neon: **sealed knit + weather shells**  
- Loom: heavy insulated stormwear, survival yarn culture  
**Flavor:** quiet sturdiness; materials feel like they were chosen by weather.

---

### **Marrakesh / Desert Prism Markets**
**Climate problem:** sun, dust, temp swings.  
- linen, cotton wraps, light leather  
- **dust-resistant weaves**, bright dyes  
- bead-metal jewelry that doubles as status + barter  
- Neon: layered wraps with reinforced inner panels  
- Loom: **scarf-masks, waxed light coats, rope belts**  
**Flavor:** saturated color + practical airflow.

---

### **Santa Fe Scar / High Desert Corridor**
**Climate problem:** mirage heat + dust + sudden fronts.  
- suede, denim, canvas  
- turquoise/copper cultural accents  
- Neon: **patch-panel jackets** with dust masks built-in  
- Loom: **storm-rated ponchos**, goggles, thick sole boots  
**Flavor:** sun-bleached toughness, visible survival chic.

---

### **Honey Island / Swamp Corridors**
**Climate problem:** wet brush + insects + unstable ground.  
- waxed canvas, rubber boots  
- rope nets, waterproof pouches  
- in Loom: **insect-mesh veils, oilskin squares**  
**Flavor:** everything looks water-smart and hush-ready.

---

## Ultra-quick material tells
Use these as one-line texture hooks:
- **Veil:** “clean seams, real buttons, old brass.”  
- **Neon:** “stitched ribs, patched panels, scuffed acrylic.”  
- **Loom:** “waxed canvas armor, quilted warmth, rope integrated.”  
- **Post:** “repairs kept as memory; fabrics soften.”

# 2) SAGA-FIT AUDIT (Track-1 Systems vs current Concord Saga)

Here’s a hard, grounded audit of fit and any stress-points.

## What fits extremely well
### **A) UARS + Weather + VFX alignment**
- UARS says resonance is **performance-edge, not magic**.  
- Weather/VFX make resonance **sensory/pressure-based**.  
- Both scale with trilogy collapse.  
**Result:** action scenes feel credible, and visuals won’t drift into superhero territory.

### **B) Tech downshift ladder**
AR/Holo/Comms degrade predictably by rung and trilogy.  
**Result:** no “why didn’t they just call X?” holes.

### **C) Transportation ecology**
Train/liner/airship safety logic matches:
- Veil normalcy  
- Neon corridor reliance  
- Loom hurricane-chasing risk  
**Result:** travel beats are constrained in a satisfying way.

### **D) Light economy → civic order**
Your barter pillars naturally birth Knots/convoys/Concord legitimacy.  
**Result:** governance emerges *organically* without exposition.

### **E) Coolness target**
“Effortless cool” comes from:
- visible ingenuity at low tiers  
- calm competence at mid tiers  
- cultural gravity at prestige tiers  
**Result:** matches your tone goal and avoids sci-fi swagger.

---

## Watch-outs / likely future friction
### **1) “Violence opt-out” risk**
Your world systems allow de-escalation, but chaos still needs teeth.
- **Fix:** make scarcity breaches or calm-protocol failures *force short violence bursts* (UARS style), not prolonged warfare.  
- This keeps your low-violence identity while staying believable.

### **2) Green / blue readability**
Hybrid-final green = structural strain, blue = clarity.  
- **Risk:** if overused, readers may not intuit green ≠ safe.  
- **Fix:** in prose, pair green with *strain verbs*: “tight,” “loaded,” “brittle,” “processing.”  
  Pair blue with *relief verbs*: “exhale,” “settle,” “clean,” “true.”

### **3) Prestige travel logistics**
You’ve got cool set pieces (Orient-Express trains, airships, liners).  
- **Risk:** readers may ask why everyone doesn’t flee on them.  
- **Fix:** economize through **credential + contribution** gates (already canon).  
  Make sanctuary seats **rare and socially negotiated**.

### **4) MT prominence under tech collapse**
MT in Loom is “nearly unwatchable.”  
- **Risk:** MT might fade too hard if not scaffolded.  
- **Fix:** keep MT alive via **audio bursts + dead-drops** and Kade’s human voice, not visuals.

None of these are contradictions — just tuning points.

---

## Audit verdict
**System fit is strong and thematically coherent.**  
You can proceed to beat bibles confidently. The main tuning lever later is **how often scarcity/calm failure triggers brief violence** to keep stakes real.

---

# 3) BIG CONTEXT HOLES (governments, religion, etc.)

These are the “ambient world questions” that will show up in readers’ heads. You don’t need full bibles, just **credible lanes**.

---

## A) What are governments doing during all this?
### Veil
- **Official posture:** “anomalies are infrastructure events.”  
- Mix of denial + quiet response teams + Dominion pressure.  
- Governments focus on **stability optics**, not truth.

### Neon
Governments vary by region, but three believable modes:
1) **Grid-control states (Technarch-leaning):**  
   attempt damping rigs, curfews, surveillance, “calibration police.”  
   → lose legitimacy as folk corridors outperform them.

2) **Adaptive coalition states:**  
   partner with elders, med councils, Concord, rail crews.  
   → survive longer, become model cities.

3) **Fractured/failed states:**  
   splinter into militia zones, black markets, Choirless sanctuaries.  
   → become plot pressure but not the saga’s center.

### Loom
- Most national governments become **shells or symbols**.  
- Real authority migrates to:
  - Knots  
  - corridors  
  - Concord stabilizer federation  
- Some remnants exist only to **issue passports/credentials** or hoard stockpiles.

**Reader-intuitive line:**  
“Governments didn’t vanish. They went local.”

---

## B) Organized religion / spirituality
Important to handle *human-first, no validation of magic*.

### Veil
- Traditional religions behave normally.  
- Small fringe groups interpret ghostwaves as signs — mostly ignored.

### Neon
Religion splinters into:
1) **Stability faiths:**  
   rituals for calm etiquette, grief, community.  
   → often align with Filaments.

2) **Apocalyptic sects:**  
   interpret blooms as judgment / cleansing.  
   → recruitment spikes in fear zones.

3) **Anti-resonance doctrines:**  
   dovetail with Choirless logic (“emotion is entropy”).  
   → become dangerous when weaponized.

### Loom
- **Ritual becomes civic tech.**  
- Faith leaders often become **calm-protocol anchors** or med-grief facilitators.  
- Some sects collapse; others become the moral spine of Knots.

**Constraint:**  
No religion is “right” about cosmology. Their power is social/emotional.

---

## C) Education, science, and archives
### Veil
- Official science studies anomalies quietly.  
- Dominion archives hoard data.

### Neon
- Citizen science explodes (MT + corridor watchers).  
- Universities fracture into:
  - Technarch labs (control)  
  - folk labs (observe/report)  
- Competing narratives fuel tension.

### Loom
- **Archives go analog and sacred.**  
- Witness ledgers + paper libraries become survival memory.  
- Reykjavik/Vienna-style archive pockets gain plot weight.

---

## D) Economy-religion-government intersections (nice plot fuel)
- Governments use religion for calm legitimacy.  
- Apocalyptic groups disrupt ration fairness.  
- Choirless sanctuaries market emotional suppression as “holy calm.”  
- Filament rituals outperform institutions in keeping people human.  
- Concord stays deliberately *non-theocratic* and trust-based.

---

## E) Other ambient holes (quick lanes)
- **International relations:** become corridor alliances, not UN summits.  
- **Big corporations:** either Technarch adjuncts or salvage guilds.  
- **Media besides MT:** Chronicle/Gazette become sober recorders when MT gets chaotic.  
- **Prisons / justice:** shrink to Knot councils and restitution; Dominion zones go punitive.

---

- Weather/VFX

# 1) FULL CONTEXT INTEGRITY SWEEP (Möbius-style)

I’m auditing for: **Humanity-pass, Breadcrumb-pass, Resonance-logic pass, Antagonist-pressure pass, Emotional-circuit pass, Reader-intuition pass.**  
I’ll flag **locks** (good), **risks**, and **fixes**.

---

## A) Humanity-Pass (do systems keep readers inside people?)
### Locks
- **Resonance is not magic.** RO amplifies human edges; cost is felt.  
- **Weather is bodily + social**, not cosmic fireworks.  
- **Tech downshifts rather than deus-ex-machinas.**  
- **Economy + civic order are human-scale** (Knots, pods, councils).  
- **Fashion/materials show survival + culture**, not sci-fi uniforms.

### Risks
1) **Over-systematizing in prose.**  
   Too many named systems could feel like a manual.
2) **“Competence porn” crowding emotion.**  
   If everyone always follows calm etiquette perfectly, scenes lose human mess.

### Fixes
- **Bury system names; show behavior.**  
  Use rung cues + object cues, not lecture.
- **Allow *small, earned failures* of etiquette** to create humanity:
  grief spikes, jealousy arguments, panic kids, stubborn elders.  
  The system is there to catch them, not erase them.

**Verdict:** Humanity alignment strong. Just keep jargon off-page.

---

## B) Breadcrumb-Pass (do systems feed plot without spoiling it?)
### Locks
- **Shard taxonomy** escalates cleanly.  
- **AR glitches** act as early warning breadcrumbs.  
- **MT/Chronicle split** allows tonal and info staggering.  
- **Travel corridors** give geographic breadcrumb rails.

### Risks
1) **Breadcrumb density overload in Neon.**  
   You could accidentally stack too many “signals” per beat.
2) **VT proximity visual cues too loud too early.**  
   Violet planar stuff is powerful; overuse could telegraph endgame.

### Fixes
- **One breadcrumb vector per beat.**  
  Example: either AR tear, folk board update, or sensory shimmer — not all three.
- **VT visuals stay *rare and intimate*** until late Neon / Loom.  
  Let the reader *miss* it sometimes.

**Verdict:** Breadcrumb logic healthy with a “one-signal-per-beat” discipline.

---

## C) Resonance-Logic Pass (physics consistency)
### Locks
- UARS equation aligns with environmental pressure + weather ladder.  
- VFX grammar ties strictly to emotion × intent × pressure.  
- MT never produces resonance visuals.  
- Metas lack Intent → instability → no ascension.

### Risks
1) **Green/blue semantics drift.**  
   You may unconsciously write green as “safe.”
2) **Prismatic Loom moments turning “pretty.”**  
   Loom visuals must feel dangerous/meaningful, not aesthetic spectacle.
3) **Transportation safety vs speed edge cases.**  
   Readers may ask why slow travel is always safer (not always true IRL).

### Fixes
- **Green verbs = strain verbs; blue verbs = relief verbs.**  
- **Prismatic = cost, nausea, drag, terror** unless it’s the Mending itself.  
- **Safety is about stability windows, not speed alone.**  
  Add one line occasionally: “planes in a front shear themselves; slow ships can ride the lull.”

**Verdict:** Resonance logic solid. Minor semantic guardrails needed.

---

## D) Antagonist-Pressure Pass (do threats feel real in this system?)
### Locks
- Antagonists are human/human-made.  
- Scarcity + ideology create believable escalation.  
- Opponent archetypes map to civic/economic collapse perfectly.  
- Choirless / Hardliners / Dominions each have *different pressure vectors*.

### Risks
1) **Low-violence tone vs chaos realism.**  
   Too much de-escalation can feel unearned in Neon/Loom.
2) **Manufactured metas risk “final boss syndrome.”**  
   If they show up too often, they feel like monsters not tools.

### Fixes
- **Violence is *brief, ugly, costly* and triggered by system failure.**  
  This keeps tone “opt-out” while staying credible.  
- **Metas appear sparingly and consequentially.**  
  Their presence should *always* change the room.

**Verdict:** Threat pressure is appropriate with scarcity-triggered violence beats.

---

## E) Emotional-Circuit Pass (do systems reinforce arcs?)
### Locks
- Weather = externalized emotional pressure.  
- VFX overrides map cleanly to character arcs.  
- Kits/economy/civic rituals all *reward empathy and Intent*.  
- Travel set pieces allow grief/hope/identity contrasts.

### Risks
1) **Ritual safety reading as “too clean.”**  
   If rituals always work, emotional growth plateaus.
2) **AR personalization flattening shared emotional stakes.**  
   If everyone lives in their own skin all the time, communal moments weaken.

### Fixes
- **Rituals can fail under mismatch/dissonance.**  
  That failure becomes character work.  
- **In peaks, AR collapses → shared raw reality.**  
  That’s where group emotion lands hardest.

**Verdict:** Emotional circuit is strong and naturally enforced by tech/weather.

---

## F) Reader-Intuition Pass (do readers “get it” instantly?)
### Locks
- Five-pocket kit, rung scripts, and glyphs are intuitive.  
- Tech downshift mirrors real-world expectations.  
- Social order reads like disaster logic we’ve seen before.  
- Materials/fabrics are climate-based and obvious.

### Risks
1) **Multiple ladders (intensity scale + rungs) could confuse you, not the reader.**
2) **Too many proper nouns (Knot, Corridor, Blue-line, Quiet-car, etc.).**

### Fixes
- **On-page you only show rungs indirectly.**  
  Intensity scale is internal only.  
- **Cap proper nouns per chapter.**  
  If a chapter already uses “Knot,” don’t add new term unless necessary.

**Verdict:** Reader intuition high as long as you keep noun-count low.

---

## OVERALL INTEGRITY VERDICT
**The Track-1 context stack is coherent, cross-locked, and saga-fit.**  
Your main operating disciplines going forward:

1) **No jargon on page.**  
2) **One breadcrumb vector per beat.**  
3) **Violence only when systems fail — brief, costly.**  
4) **Green=processing strain; Blue/White=relief/filtration.**  
5) **Cap proper nouns.**

If you follow those, drift risk stays low.

# 2) GLOBAL SANDBOX BOUNDARIES (Big-picture “world is / isn’t”)

These are the **guardrails** you can hand to any collaborator or future-you.

---

## A) What this world *is*
1) **Near-modern Earth with an emotional-physics layer.**  
   Tech level is late-2010s/2020s; culture is recognizable.

2) **Resonance is a human-performance ecology, not a power fantasy.**  
   It amplifies the *upper edge of plausible human ability*.

3) **Collapse is civic and emotional, not alien or supernatural.**  
   Institutions fail; humans improvise.

4) **Visual awe comes from emotion + environment + contrast.**  
   Not from tech spectacle.

5) **Hope is procedural, not magical.**  
   People survive by fairness, empathy, and Intent.

---

## B) What this world *is not*
1) **Not a sci-fi future.**  
   No starships, no ubiquitous automation, no sleek utopia tech.

2) **Not superhero fantasy.**  
   No energy blasts, telekinesis, healing, flight, physics breaks.

3) **Not a cosmology-as-religion story.**  
   Faith matters socially, but no institution is “right” about the Veil.

4) **Not a war saga.**  
   Violence exists, but the saga’s center is **emotional/civic survival**.

5) **Not a tech-explainer series.**  
   Readers intuit through behavior + sensory cues.

---

5) **No purple-prose magic language for resonance.**
